has exactly two critical points, one is a local minimum at negative level, the other one is a global maximum at positive level. Further, there exists
0<R_{0}<R_{1}
such that
h(R_{0})=h(R_{1})=0
,
h(t)>0
if and only if

t\in(R_{0},R_{1})
.

Proof.

Since

h(t)=t^{\frac{3}{2}}\Big[\frac{1}{2}t^{\frac{1}{2}}-\frac{1}{4}C^{4}_{3,4}(\mu_{1}b_{1}+\mu_{2}b_{2}+\rho b^{\frac{1}{2}}_{1}b^{\frac{1}{2}}_{2})t^{\frac{3}{2}}-\frac{1}{2}|\beta|\big(\frac{2}{3}b^{\frac{3}{2}}_{1}+\frac{1}{3}b^{\frac{3}{2}}_{2}\big)C^{3}_{3,3}\Big]

it is easy to see that
h(t)>0
if and only if
\varphi(t)>\frac{1}{2}|\beta|\big(\frac{2}{3}b^{\frac{3}{2}}_{1}+\frac{1}{3}b^{\frac{3}{2}}_{2}\big)C^{3}_{3,3}
, where
\varphi(t)=\frac{1}{2}t^{\frac{1}{2}}-\frac{1}{4}C^{4}_{3,4}(\mu_{1}b_{1}+\mu_{2}b_{2}+\rho b^{\frac{1}{2}}_{1}b^{\frac{1}{2}}_{2})t^{\frac{3}{2}}
, since

\varphi^{\prime}(t)=\frac{1}{4}t^{-\frac{1}{2}}-\frac{3}{8}C^{4}_{3,4}(\mu_{1}b_{1}+\mu_{2}b_{2}+\rho b^{\frac{1}{2}}_{1}b^{\frac{1}{2}}_{2})t^{\frac{1}{2}},

it is easy to see that
\varphi(t)
has a unique global maximum point at positive level in
\widetilde{t}=\frac{2}{3C^{4}_{3,4}(\mu_{1}b_{1}+\mu_{2}b_{1}+\rho b^{\frac{1}{2}}_{1}b^{\frac{1}{2}}_{2})}
and

\varphi(\widetilde{t})=\frac{\sqrt{6}}{9}\frac{1}{\sqrt{C^{4}_{3,4}(\mu_{1}b_{1}+\mu_{2}b_{2}+\rho b^{\frac{1}{2}}_{1}b^{\frac{1}{2}}_{2})}},

therefore
h(t)
is positive on an open interval
(R_{0},R_{1})
if and only if
\varphi(\widetilde{t})>\frac{1}{2}|\beta|\left(\frac{2}{3}b^{\frac{3}{2}}_{1}+\frac{1}{3}b^{\frac{3}{2}}_{2}\right)C^{3}_{3,3}
, so

\displaystyle|\beta|\big(2b^{\frac{3}{2}}_{1}+b^{\frac{3}{2}}_{2}\big)C^{3}_{3,3}C^{2}_{3,4}\sqrt{\mu_{1}b_{1}+\mu_{2}b_{2}+\rho b^{\frac{1}{2}}_{1}b^{\frac{1}{2}}_{2}}<\frac{2\sqrt{6}}{3}.

(4.10)

Since

\displaystyle h^{\prime}(t)

\displaystyle=t^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big[t^{\frac{1}{2}}-\frac{3}{4}C^{4}_{3,4}(\mu_{1}b_{1}+\mu_{2}b_{2}+\rho b^{\frac{1}{2}}_{1}b^{\frac{1}{2}}_{2})t^{\frac{3}{2}}-\frac{3}{4}|\beta|\big(\frac{2}{3}b^{\frac{3}{2}}_{1}+\frac{1}{3}b^{\frac{3}{2}}_{2}\big)C^{3}_{3,3}\Big]=t^{\frac{1}{2}}g(t),

we can deduce that when

\displaystyle g(\frac{4}{9C^{4}_{3,4}(\mu_{1}b_{1}+\mu_{2}b_{1}+\rho b^{\frac{1}{2}}_{1}b^{\frac{1}{2}}_{2})})=\frac{4}{9\sqrt{C^{4}_{3,4}(\mu_{1}b_{1}+\mu_{2}b_{1}+\rho b^{\frac{1}{2}}_{1}b^{\frac{1}{2}}_{2})}}-\frac{3}{4}|\beta|\big(\frac{2}{3}b^{\frac{3}{2}}_{1}+\frac{1}{3}b^{\frac{3}{2}}_{2}\big)C^{3}_{3,3}>0,

(4.11)

then
h(t)
has exactly two critical points, one is a local minimum at negative level, the other one is a global maximum at positive level. It is easy to see that when (
4.10
) holds, then (
4.11
) also holds.
∎

Let

N:=\Big\{(u,v)\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{4})\times H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{4})~|~\beta\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}u^{2}vdx>0\Big\},

and for simplicity, we still denote

\mathcal{P}_{b_{1},b_{2}}=\Big\{(u,v)\in(\mathrm{T}_{b_{1}}\times\mathrm{T}_{b_{2}})\cap N~|~P_{b_{1},b_{2}}(u,v)=0\Big\}.

Lemma 4.3
.

Under the assumption (
4.10
), then
\mathcal{P}^{0}_{b_{1},b_{2}}=\emptyset
and
\mathcal{P}_{b_{1},b_{2}}
is a
C^{1}
submanifold in
H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\times H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
with codimension 3.

Proof.

Assume by contradiction that there exists a
(u,v)\in\mathcal{P}^{0}_{b_{1},b_{2}}
such that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla u|^{2}+|\nabla v|^{2})dx=\frac{3}{4}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(\mu_{1}u^{4}+\mu_{2}v^{4}+2\rho u^{2}v^{2})dx+\frac{3}{4}\beta\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}u^{2}vdx

(4.12)

and

\displaystyle 2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla u|^{2}+|\nabla v|^{2})dx-\frac{9\beta}{8}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}u^{2}vdx-\frac{9}{4}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left(\mu_{1}u^{4}+\mu_{2}v^{4}+2\rho u^{2}v^{2}\right)dx=0.

(4.13)

From (
4.12
) and (
4.13
), we obtain

\displaystyle\beta\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}u^{2}vdx=2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left(\mu_{1}u^{4}+\mu_{2}v^{4}+2\rho u^{2}v^{2}\right)dx.

(4.14)

Thus, from (
2.1
)-(
2.3
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla u|^{2}+|\nabla v|^{2})dx

\displaystyle=\frac{9}{4}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left(\mu_{1}u^{4}+\mu_{2}v^{4}+2\rho u^{2}v^{2}\right)dx

(4.15)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{9\mu_{1}}{4}C^{4}_{3,4}b_{1}\|\nabla u\|^{3}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\frac{9\mu_{2}}{4}C^{4}_{3,4}b_{2}\|\nabla v\|^{3}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{9}{4}\rho C^{4}_{3,4}b^{\frac{1}{2}}_{1}b^{\frac{1}{2}}_{2}\left[\|\nabla u\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\|\nabla v\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\right]^{\frac{3}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{9}{4}C^{4}_{3,4}(\mu_{1}b_{1}+\mu_{2}b_{2}+\rho b^{\frac{1}{2}}_{1}b^{\frac{1}{2}}_{2})\left[\|\nabla u\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\|\nabla v\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\right]^{\frac{3}{2}},

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla u|^{2}+|\nabla v|^{2})dx

\displaystyle=\frac{9}{8}\beta\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}u^{2}vdx

(4.16)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{9}{8}|\beta|\left[\left(\frac{2}{3}b^{\frac{3}{2}}_{1}+\frac{1}{3}b^{\frac{3}{2}}_{2}\right)C^{3}_{3,3}\left[\|\nabla u\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\|\nabla v\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\right]^{\frac{3}{4}}\right].

So, by (
4.15
) and (
4.16
), we get

\frac{1}{3\sqrt{C^{4}_{3,4}(\mu_{1}b_{1}+\mu_{2}b_{1}+\rho b^{\frac{1}{2}}_{1}b^{\frac{1}{2}}_{2})}}\leq\frac{9}{16}|\beta|\mathcal{D}_{4},

therefore

\displaystyle|\beta|\left(2b^{\frac{3}{2}}_{1}+b^{\frac{3}{2}}_{2}\right)C^{3}_{3,3}C^{2}_{3,4}\sqrt{\mu_{1}b_{1}+\mu_{2}b_{2}+\rho b^{\frac{1}{2}}_{1}b^{\frac{1}{2}}_{2}}\geq\frac{16}{9},

(4.17)

contract the assumption (
4.10
), which implies that
\mathcal{P}^{0}_{b_{1},b_{2}}=\emptyset
. Next, we show that
\mathcal{P}_{b_{1},b_{2}}
is a smooth manifold of codimension 3 on
H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\times H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
. It is easy to see that
\mathcal{P}_{b_{1},b_{2}}
is defined by
P_{b_{1},b_{2}}(u,v)=0
,
G(u)=0
,
F(u)=0
and
E(u,v)>0
, where

G(u)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}u^{2}dx-b^{2}_{1},\ \ F(v)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}v^{2}dx-b^{2}_{2}\ \ \text{and}\ \ E(u,v)=\beta\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}u^{2}v.

Since
P_{b_{1},b_{2}}(u,v)
,
G(u)
,
F(u)
and
E(u,v)
are class of
C^{1}
, we only need to check that

d(P_{b_{1},b_{2}}(u,v),G(u),F(v),E(u,v)):H\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{3}\ \text{ is surjective},

for every
(u,v)\in(G^{-1}(0)\times F^{-1}(0))\cap E^{-1}(0)\cap P^{-1}_{b_{1},b_{2}}(0)
.
If this is not true,
dP_{b_{1},b_{2}}(u,v)
has to be linearly dependent from
dG(u)
,
dF(v)
and
dE(u,v)
i.e. there exist
\nu_{1},\nu_{2},\nu_{3}\in\mathbb{R}
such that

\begin{cases}2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\nabla u\nabla\varphi+\nu_{1}u\varphi+\nu_{3}\beta uv\varphi=3\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(\mu_{1}u^{3}\varphi+\rho v^{2}u\varphi)+\frac{3\beta}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}u\varphi v&\text{in}\ \ \mathbb{R}^{3},\\
2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\nabla v\nabla\psi+\nu_{2}v\psi+\frac{\nu_{3}}{2}\beta u^{2}\psi=3\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(\mu_{2}v^{3}\psi+\rho u^{2}v\psi)+\frac{3\beta}{4}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}u^{2}\psi&\text{in}\ \ \mathbb{R}^{3},\end{cases}

for every
(\varphi,\psi)\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\times H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
, so

\begin{cases}-2[\Delta u+\nu_{1}u+\nu_{3}\beta uv]=3(\mu_{1}u^{3}+\rho v^{2}u)+\frac{3\beta}{2}uv&\text{in}\ \ \mathbb{R}^{3},\\
-2[\Delta v+\nu_{2}v+\frac{\nu_{3}}{2}\beta u^{2}]=3(\mu_{2}v^{3}+\rho u^{2}v)+\frac{3\beta}{4}u^{2}&\text{in}\ \ \mathbb{R}^{3}.\end{cases}

The Pohozaev identity for above system is

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla u|^{2}+|\nabla v|^{2})dx-\frac{9}{8}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left(\mu_{1}u^{4}+\mu_{2}v^{4}+2\rho u^{2}v^{2}\right)dx-\frac{3}{2}\big(\frac{3}{8}-\frac{\nu_{3}}{2}\big)\beta\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}u^{2}vdx=0.

Then
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla u|^{2}+|\nabla v|^{2})dx=\frac{9}{8}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left(\mu_{1}u^{4}+\mu_{2}v^{4}+2\rho u^{2}v^{2}\right)dx
.
Since
(u,v)\in(G^{-1}(0)\times F^{-1}(0))\cap E^{-1}(0)\cap P^{-1}_{b_{1},b_{2}}(0)
, we have

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla u|^{2}+|\nabla v|^{2})dx=\frac{3}{4}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left(\mu_{1}u^{4}+\mu_{2}v^{4}+2\rho u^{2}v^{2}\right)dx
, this is a contradiction. By Proposition A.1 of
[
33
]
, we get that if
m_{\beta}:=\inf\limits_{(u,v)\in\mathcal{P}_{b_{1},b_{2}}}J_{\beta}=J_{\beta}(\bar{u},\bar{v})
, then there exist
\lambda_{i}\in\mathbb{R}(i=4)
, such that

\displaystyle-(1+\lambda_{4})\Delta\bar{u}+\lambda_{1}\nu_{1}\bar{u}+\lambda_{3}\beta\bar{u}\bar{v}=(1+\frac{3\lambda_{4}}{2})(\mu_{1}\bar{u}^{3}+\rho\bar{v}^{2}u)+(1+\frac{3\lambda_{4}}{4})\beta\bar{u}\bar{v},

\displaystyle-(1+\lambda_{4})\Delta\bar{v}+\lambda_{2}\bar{v}+\frac{\lambda_{3}}{2}\beta\bar{u}^{2}=(1+\frac{3\lambda_{4}}{2}\lambda_{4})(\mu_{2}\bar{v}^{3}+\rho\bar{u}^{2}\bar{v})+(1+\frac{3\lambda_{4}}{4})\frac{\beta}{2}\bar{u}^{2}.

Since
E(\bar{u},\bar{v})>0
, from Theorem 1 of
[
13
]
or the proof of Lemma 2.11 in
[
33
]
, we have
\lambda_{3}=0
. Therefore, we obtain that the restricted set
N
does not change the structure of the manifold
\mathcal{P}_{b_{1},b_{2}}
. Then, we have that
\mathcal{P}_{b_{1},b_{2}}
is a smooth manifold of codimension 3 on
\mathrm{T}_{b_{1}}\times\mathrm{T}_{b_{2}}
.
∎

Lemma 4.4
.

When
\beta\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}u^{2}vdx>0
. For every
(u,v)\in\mathrm{T}_{b_{1}}\times\mathrm{T}_{b_{2}}
, the function
\Psi_{u,v}(t)
has exactly two critical points
s_{u,v}<t_{u,v}\in\mathbb{R}
and two zeros
c_{u,v}<d_{u,v}\in\mathbb{R}
with
s_{u,v}<c_{u,v}<t_{u,v}<d_{u,v}
. Moreover,

(1)

s_{u,v}\star(u,v)\in\mathcal{P}^{+}_{b_{1},b_{2}}(u,v)
and
t_{u,v}\star(u,v)\in\mathcal{P}^{-}_{b_{1},b_{2}}(u,v)
, and if
t\star(u,v)\in\mathcal{P}_{b_{1},b_{2}}(u,v)
, then either
t=s_{u,v}
or
t=t_{u,v}.

(2)

(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla(t\star u)|^{2}+|\nabla(t\star v)|^{2})dx)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq R_{0}
for every
t\leq c_{u,v},
and

\displaystyle J_{\beta}(u,v)(s_{u,v}\star(u,v))

\displaystyle=\min\bigg\{J_{\beta}(t\star(u,v):t\in\mathbb{R}\ \text{and}\ (\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla(t\star u)|^{2}+|\nabla(t\star v)|^{2})dx)^{\frac{1}{2}}<R_{0}\bigg\}<0,

where
R_{0}
is defined in Lemma
4.2
.

(3)

We have

J_{\beta}(t_{u,v}\star(u,v))=\max\{J_{\beta}(t\star(u,v)):t\in\mathbb{R}\}>0

and
\Psi_{u,v}(t)
is strictly decreasing and concave on
(t_{u,v},+\infty)
. In particular, if
t_{u,v}<0
, then
P_{b_{1},b_{2}}(u,v)<0.

(4)

The maps
(u,v)\in\mathrm{T}_{b_{1}}\times\mathrm{T}_{b_{2}}:s_{u,v}\in\mathbb{R}
and
(u,v)\in\mathrm{T}_{b_{1}}\times\mathrm{T}_{b_{2}}:t_{u,v}\in\mathbb{R}
are of class
C^{1}
.

Proof.

Let
(u,v)\in\mathrm{T}_{b_{1}}\times\mathrm{T}_{b_{2}}
, since
t\star(u,v)\in\mathcal{P}_{b_{1},b_{2}}(u,v)
if and only if
(\Psi_{u,v})^{\prime}(t)=0
. Thus, we first show that
\Psi_{u,v}(t)
has at least two critical points. From (
4.9
), we have

\displaystyle\Psi_{u,v}(t)

\displaystyle=J_{\beta}(t\star(u,v))\geq h\Big(\big[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla(t\star u)|^{2}+|\nabla(t\star v)|^{2})dx\big]^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big)

\displaystyle=h\Big(e^{t}\big[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla u|^{2}+|\nabla v|^{2})dx\big])^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big).

Thus, the
C^{2}
function
\Psi_{u,v}(t)
is positive on

\Big(\ln\frac{R_{0}}{\big[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla u|^{2}+|\nabla v|^{2})dx\big]^{\frac{1}{2}}},\ln\frac{R_{1}}{\big[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla u|^{2}+|\nabla v|^{2})dx\big]^{\frac{1}{2}}}\Big),

\Psi_{u,v}(+\infty)=-\infty
and
\Psi_{u,v}(-\infty)=0^{-}
.

Indeed,
(\Psi_{u,v})^{\prime}(t)=0
implies that
g(t)=\frac{3\beta}{4}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}u^{2}vdx
where

g(t)=e^{\frac{t}{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla u|^{2}+|\nabla v|^{2})dx-\frac{3e^{\frac{3t}{2}}}{4}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left(\mu_{1}u^{4}+\mu_{2}v^{4}+2\rho u^{2}v^{2}\right)dx.

It is easy to see that
g(t)
has a unique maximum point, thus the above equation has at most two solutions.
If
\frac{3\beta}{4}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}u^{2}vdx>0
. So,
\Psi_{u,v}(-\infty)=0^{-}
,
\Psi_{u,v}(+\infty)=-\infty
.
It is easy to see that
\Psi_{u,v}(t)
has a local minimum point
s_{u,v}
at negative level in
(0,\ln\frac{R_{0}}{\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla u|^{2}+|\nabla v|^{2})dx\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}})
and has a global maximum point
t_{u,v}
at positive level in
\Big(\ln\frac{R_{0}}{\big[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla u|^{2}+|\nabla v|^{2})dx\big]^{\frac{1}{2}}},\ln\frac{R_{1}}{\big[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla u|^{2}+|\nabla v|^{2})dx\big]^{\frac{1}{2}}}\Big).
Next, we show that
\Psi_{u,v}(t)
has no other critical points. From
(u,v)\in\mathrm{T}_{b_{1}}\times\mathrm{T}_{b_{2}},

t\in\mathbb{R}
is a critical point of
\Psi_{u,v}(t)
if and only if
t\star(u,v)\in\mathcal{P}_{b_{1},b_{2}},
we have
s_{u,v}\star(u,v),\ \ t_{u,v}\star(u,v)\in\mathcal{P}_{b_{1}\times b_{2}}
and
t\star(u,v)\in\mathcal{P}_{b_{1}\times b_{2}}
if and only if
t=s_{u,v}
or
t=t_{u,v}
. Since
s_{u,v}
is a local minimum point of
\Psi_{u,v}(t)
, we know that
(\Psi_{u,v})^{\prime\prime}(s_{u,v})\geq 0
, from
\mathcal{P}^{0}_{b_{1},b_{2}}=\emptyset
, we know that
(\Psi_{u,v})^{\prime\prime}(s_{u,v})\neq 0
, thus
(\Psi_{u,v})^{\prime\prime}(s_{u,v})>0
, therefore
s_{u,v}\star(u,v)\in\mathcal{P}^{+}_{b_{1},b_{2}}.
Similarly, we have
t_{u,v}\star(u,v)\in\mathcal{P}^{-}_{b_{1},b_{2}}.
By the monotonicity and the behavior at infinity of
\Psi_{u,v}(t)
, we know that
\Psi_{u,v}(t)
has exactly two zeros
c_{u,v}<d_{u,v}
with
s_{u,v}<c_{u,v}<t_{u,v}<d_{u,v}
and
\Psi_{u,v}(t)
has exactly two inflection points, in particular,
\Psi_{u,v}(t)
is concave on
[t_{u,v},+\infty)
and hence if
t_{u,v}<0
, then
P_{b_{1},b_{2}}(u,v)=(\Psi_{u,v})^{\prime}(0)<0.
Finally, we prove that
(u,v)\in\mathrm{T}_{b_{1}}\times\mathrm{T}_{b_{2}}\mapsto s_{u,v}\in\mathbb{R}
and
(u,v)\in\mathrm{T}_{b_{1}}\times\mathrm{T}_{b_{2}}\mapsto t_{u,v}\in\mathbb{R}
are of class
C^{1}
. Indeed, we can apply the implicit function theorem on the
C^{1}
function
\Phi(t,u,v)=(\Psi_{u,v})^{\prime}(t)
, then
\Phi(s_{u,v},u,v)=(\Psi_{u,v})^{\prime}(s_{u,v})=0,\partial_{s}\Phi(s_{u,v},u,v)=(\Psi_{u,v})^{\prime\prime}(s_{u,v})<0
, thus
(u,v)\in\mathrm{T}_{b_{1}}\times\mathrm{T}_{b_{2}}\mapsto s_{u,v}\in\mathbb{R}
is class of
C^{1}
. Similarly, we can prove that
(u,v)\in\mathrm{T}_{b_{1}}\times\mathrm{T}_{b_{2}}\mapsto t_{u,v}\in\mathbb{R}
is class of
C^{1}
.
∎

4.1
Existence of a local minimizer

For
k>0
, set

A_{k}=\Big\{(u,v)\in\mathrm{T}_{b_{1}}\times\mathrm{T}_{b_{2}}:\big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla u|^{2}+|\nabla v|^{2})dx\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}<k\Big\},

and

m^{+}_{\beta}(b_{1},b_{2})=\inf_{(u,v)\in A_{R_{0}}}J_{\beta}(u,v).

From Lemma
4.4
, we have following corollary

Corollary 4.1
.

The set
\mathcal{P}^{+}_{b_{1},b_{2}}
is contained in

A_{R_{0}}=\Big\{(u,v)\in\mathrm{T}_{b_{1}}\times\mathrm{T}_{b_{2}}:\big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla u|^{2}+|\nabla v|^{2})dx\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}<R_{0}\Big\}

and

\sup_{\mathcal{P}^{+}_{b_{1},b_{2}}}J_{\beta}(u,v)\leq 0\leq\inf_{\mathcal{P}^{-}_{b_{1},b_{2}}}J_{\beta}(u,v).

Lemma 4.5
.

We have
m^{+}_{\beta}(b_{1},b_{2})\in(-\infty,0)
that

m^{+}_{\beta}(b_{1},b_{2})=\inf_{\mathcal{P}_{b_{1},b_{2}}}J_{\beta}(u,v)=\inf_{\mathcal{P}^{+}_{b_{1},b_{2}}}J_{\beta}(u,v)\ \text{and}\ m^{+}_{\beta}(b_{1},b_{2})<\inf_{\overline{A_{R_{0}}}\setminus A_{R_{0}-\rho}}J_{\beta}(u,v).

Proof.

For
(u,v)\in A_{R_{0}}
, we have

J_{\beta}(u,v)\geq h\Big(\big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla u|^{2}+|\nabla v|^{2})dx\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big)\geq\min_{t\in[0,R_{0}]}h(t)>-\infty.

Therefore,
m^{+}_{\beta}(b_{1},b_{2})>-\infty.
Moreover, there exists
(u,v)\in\mathrm{T}_{b_{1}}\times\mathrm{T}_{b_{2}}
, such that

\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla(t\star u)|^{2}+|\nabla(t\star v)|^{2})dx\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}<R_{0}

and
J_{\beta}(t\star(u,v))<0
for
t\ll-1
. Hence
m^{+}_{\beta}(b_{1},b_{2})<0.
Since
\mathcal{P}^{+}_{b_{1},b_{2}}\subset A_{R_{0}}
, we know that
m^{+}_{\beta}(b_{1},b_{2})\leq\inf_{\mathcal{P}^{+}_{b_{1},b_{2}}}J_{\beta}(u,v).
On the other hand, if
(u,v)\in A_{R_{0}}
, then
s_{u,v}\star(u,v)\in\mathcal{P}^{+}_{b_{1},b_{2}}\subset A_{R_{0}}
and

\displaystyle J_{\beta}(u,v)(s_{u,v}\star(u,v))=\min\Big\{J_{\beta}

\displaystyle(t\star(u,v)):t\in\mathbb{R}\ \text{and}\ \big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla(t\star u)|^{2}+|\nabla(t\star v)|^{2})dx\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}<R_{0}\Big\}

\displaystyle\leq J_{\beta}(u,v),

where
R_{0}
is defined in Lemma
4.2
, so
\inf_{\mathcal{P}^{+}_{b_{1},b_{2}}}J_{\beta}(u,v)\leq m^{+}_{\beta}(b_{1},b_{2})
. Since
J_{\beta}(u,v)>0
on
\mathcal{P}^{-}_{b_{1},b_{2}}
, we know that
\inf_{\mathcal{P}^{+}_{b_{1},b_{2}}}J_{\beta}(u,v)=\inf_{\mathcal{P}_{b_{1},b_{2}}}J_{\beta}(u,v).
Finally, by the continuity of
h
, there exists
\rho>0
such that
h(t)\geq\frac{m^{+}_{\beta}(b_{1},b_{2})}{2}
if
t\in[R_{0}-\rho,R_{0}]
.
Therefore,

J_{\beta}(u,v)\geq h\Big(\big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla u|^{2}+|\nabla v|^{2})dx\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big)\geq\frac{m^{+}_{\beta}(b_{1},b_{2})}{2}>m^{+}_{\beta}(b_{1},b_{2})

for every
(u,v)\in\mathrm{T}_{b_{1}}\times\mathrm{T}_{b_{2}}
with
R_{0}-\rho\leq\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla u|^{2}+|\nabla v|^{2})dx\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq R_{0}
. This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 4.6
.

Under the assumption (
4.10
) holds, we have

m^{+}(b_{1},b_{2})<\min\{m^{+}(b_{1},0),m^{+}(0,b_{2})\}.

Proof.

From
[
5
]
, we know that
m^{+}(0,b_{2})
can be achieved by
v^{\ast}\in\mathrm{T}_{b_{2}}
and
v^{\ast}
is radially symmetric and decreasing. We choose aproper test function
u\in\mathrm{T}_{b_{1}}
such that
(t\star u,v^{\ast})\in\mathrm{T}_{b_{1}}\times\mathrm{T}_{b_{2}}
. From Lemma
4.4
, we obtain

\displaystyle h(t)<h_{1}(t)=\frac{1}{2}t^{2}-\frac{1}{4}\mu_{2}C^{4}_{3,4}b_{1}t^{3}-\frac{1}{2}|\beta|\frac{2}{3}b^{\frac{3}{2}}_{2}C^{3}_{3,3}t^{\frac{3}{2}}.

(4.18)

By direct calculations, there exists
0<t^{\ast}<R_{0}
such that
<h_{1}(t^{\ast})=0
. From
[
41
]
, we have

m^{+}(0,b_{2})=\inf_{v\in\mathcal{P}^{+}_{0,b_{2}}}J_{\beta}(0,v)=\inf_{v\in\mathrm{T}_{b_{2}}\cap B(t^{\ast})}J_{\beta}(0,v).

Therefore, from the analysis in Lemma
4.2
, we have

\|\nabla v^{\ast}\|_{L^{2}}\leq t^{\ast}<R_{0}<\widetilde{t}=\frac{2}{3C^{4}_{3,4}(\mu_{1}b_{1}+\mu_{2}b_{1}+\rho b^{\frac{1}{2}}_{1}b^{\frac{1}{2}}_{2})}.

Since
h(R_{0})=h(R_{1})=0
and the monotonicity of
h(t)
, we deduce that
(t\star u,v^{\ast})\in\mathrm{T}_{b_{1}}\times\mathrm{T}_{b_{2}}\cap A_{R_{0}}
for
t\ll-1
, therefore,

\displaystyle m^{+}(b_{1},b_{2})

\displaystyle=\inf_{(u,v)\in\mathrm{T}_{b_{1}}\times\mathrm{T}_{b_{2}}\cap A_{R_{0}}}J(u,v)\leq J(t\star u,v^{\ast})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\nabla v^{\ast}|^{2}dx-\frac{1}{4}\mu_{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{N}}|v^{\ast}|^{4}dx-\frac{\beta}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|t\star u|^{2}(v^{\ast})dx

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{e^{2t}}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\nabla u|^{2}dx-\frac{e^{3t}}{4}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{N}}\mu_{2}u^{4}dx-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{N}}\rho|t\star u|^{2}(v^{\ast})^{2}dx

\displaystyle<J(0,v^{\ast})=m^{+}(0,b_{2}).

Similarly, we have

m^{+}(b_{1},b_{2})<m^{+}(b_{1},0).

Hence, the proof is completed.
∎

Lemma 4.7
.

Let
\{(u_{n},v_{n})\}\subset H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\times H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
be a minimizing sequence for
J_{\beta}(u,v)|_{\mathrm{T}_{b_{1}}\times\mathrm{T}_{b_{2}}}
at level
m^{+}_{\beta}(b_{1},b_{2})
. Then
\{(u_{n},v_{n})\}
is bounded in
H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\times H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
.

Proof.

Since
P_{b_{1},b_{2}}(u_{n},v_{n})\rightarrow 0
, we have

\displaystyle P_{b_{1},b_{2}}(u_{n},v_{n})=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla u_{n}|^{2}+|\nabla v_{n}|^{2})dx-\frac{3}{4}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left(\mu_{1}u_{n}^{4}+\mu_{2}v_{n}^{4}+2\rho u_{n}^{2}v_{n}^{2}\right)dx-\frac{3}{4}\beta\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}u_{n}^{2}v_{n}dx=o_{n}(1).

Thus, from (
2.3
), we have

\displaystyle J_{\beta}(u_{n},v_{n})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{6}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla u_{n}|^{2}+|\nabla v_{n}|^{2})dx-\frac{\beta}{4}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}u_{n}^{2}v_{n}dx+o_{n}(1)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{6}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla u_{n}|^{2}+|\nabla v_{n}|^{2})dx

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{|\beta|}{4}\Big[\big(\frac{2}{3}b^{\frac{3}{2}}_{1}+\frac{1}{3}b^{\frac{3}{2}}_{2}\big)C^{3}_{3,3}\big[\|\nabla u_{n}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\|\nabla v_{n}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\big]^{\frac{3}{4}}\Big].

Since
\{(u_{n},v_{n})\}
is a minimizer sequence for
J_{\beta}(u,v)|_{\mathrm{T}_{b_{1}}\times\mathrm{T}_{b_{2}}}
at level
m^{+}_{\beta}(b_{1},b_{2})
, we have
J_{\beta}(u_{n},v_{n})\leq m^{+}_{\beta}(b_{1},b_{2})+1
for
n
large. Hence

\displaystyle\frac{1}{6}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla u_{n}|^{2}+|\nabla v_{n}|^{2})dx

\displaystyle\leq\frac{|\beta|}{4}\Big[\big(\frac{2}{3}b^{\frac{3}{2}}_{1}+\frac{1}{3}b^{\frac{3}{2}}_{2}\big)C^{3}_{3,3}\big[\|\nabla u_{n}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\|\nabla v_{n}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\big]^{\frac{3}{4}}\Big]+m^{+}_{\beta}(b_{1},b_{2})+2,

so
\{(u_{n},v_{n})\}
is bounded in
H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\times H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
.
This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 4.8
.

Let
\{(u_{n},v_{n})\}\subset\mathrm{T}_{b_{1,r}}\times\mathrm{T}_{b_{2,r}}
be a nonnegative minimizing sequence for
J_{\beta}(u,v)|_{\mathrm{T}_{b_{1}}\times\mathrm{T}_{b_{2}}}
at level
m^{+}_{\beta}(b_{1},b_{2})
with additional properties
P_{b_{1},b_{2}}(u_{n},v_{n})\rightarrow 0
and
u^{-}_{n},v^{-}_{n}\rightarrow 0
a.e. in
\mathbb{R}^{3}
, then up to a subsequence
(u_{n},v_{n})\rightarrow(u,v)
in
H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\times H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
, where
(u,v)
is a positive solution of (
1.1
) for some
\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}>0.

Proof.

From Lemma
4.7
, we known that
\{(u_{n},v_{n})\}
is bounded in
H_{r}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\times H_{r}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
, thus, by the Sobolev embedding theorem, we have
H_{r}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\hookrightarrow\hookrightarrow L^{p}_{r}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
for
2<p<6
, thus there exists a
(u,v)\in H_{r}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\times H_{r}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
such that
(u_{n},v_{n})\rightharpoonup(u,v)
in
H_{r}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\times H_{r}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
,
(u_{n},v_{n})\rightarrow(u,v)
in
L^{p}_{r}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\times L^{p}_{r}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\ \ \text{for }\ 2<p<6
and
(u_{n},v_{n})\rightarrow(u,v)
a.e in
\mathbb{R}^{3}
. Hence
u,v\geq 0
are radial functions. Since
J^{\prime}\mid_{\mathrm{T}_{b_{1}}\times\mathrm{T}_{b_{2}}}(u_{n},v_{n})\rightarrow 0
, by the Lagrange multipliers rule, we know that there exists a sequence

(\lambda_{1,n},\lambda_{2,n})\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
such that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(\nabla u_{n}\nabla\varphi+\nabla v_{n}\nabla\psi)dx+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(\lambda_{1,n}u_{n}\varphi+\lambda_{2,n}v_{n}\psi)dx

(4.19)

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(\mu_{1}u_{n}^{3}\varphi+\mu_{2}v_{n}^{3}\psi+\rho v_{n}^{2}u_{n}\varphi+\rho u_{n}^{2}v_{n}\psi)dx

\displaystyle-\beta\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}u_{n}\varphi v_{n}-\frac{\beta}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}u_{n}^{2}\psi=o(1)\|(\psi,\varphi)\|_{H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\times H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\ \ \text{in}\ \ \mathbb{R}^{3},

for every
(\varphi,\psi)\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\times H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
. We claim both
\lambda_{1,n}
and
\lambda_{2,n}
are bounded sequence, and at least one of them is converging, up to a subsequence, to a strictly negative value. Indeed, we can using
(u_{n},0)
and
(0,v_{n})
as text function in (
4.19
), we have

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\lambda_{1,n}u^{2}_{n}dx=-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\nabla u_{n}|^{2}dx+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(\mu_{1}u_{n}^{4}+\rho v_{n}^{2}u^{2}_{n})dx+\beta\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}u^{2}_{n}v_{n}+o(1)\|\varphi\|_{H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})},

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\lambda_{2,n}v^{2}_{n}dx=-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\nabla v_{n}|^{2}dx+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(\mu_{2}v_{n}^{4}+\rho v_{n}^{2}u^{2}_{n})dx+\frac{\beta}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}u^{2}_{n}v_{n}+o(1)\|\psi\|_{H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})},

so

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(\lambda_{1,n}u^{2}_{n}+\lambda_{2,n}v^{2}_{n})dx

\displaystyle=-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla u_{n}|^{2}+|\nabla v_{n}|^{2})dx+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(\mu_{1}u_{n}^{4}+\mu_{2}v_{n}^{4}+2\rho v_{n}^{2}u^{2}_{n})dx

(4.20)

\displaystyle+\frac{3}{2}\beta\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}u_{n}^{2}v_{n}dx.

By (
2.1
)-(
2.3
) and the boundedness of
\{(u_{n},v_{n})\}
, we can deduce that
(\lambda_{1,n},\lambda_{2,n})
is bounded, hence up to a subsequence
(\lambda_{1,n},\lambda_{2,n})\rightarrow(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
, passing to limits in (
4.19
), we can deduce that
(u,v)
is a nonnegative solutions of (
1.1
) . Therefore

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla u|^{2}+|\nabla v|^{2})dx+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(\lambda_{1}u^{2}+\lambda_{2}v^{2})dx=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left(\mu_{1}u^{4}+\mu_{2}v^{4}+2\rho u^{2}v^{2}\right)dx+\frac{3}{2}\beta\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}u^{2}vdx.

From (
2.1
), we can get

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla u|^{2}+|\nabla v|^{2})dx+\frac{3}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(\lambda_{1}u^{2}+\lambda_{2}v^{2})dx=\frac{3}{4}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left(\mu_{1}u^{4}+\mu_{2}v^{4}+2\rho u^{2}v^{2}\right)dx+\frac{3}{2}\beta\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}u^{2}vdx.

Thus we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(\lambda_{1}u^{2}+\lambda_{2}v^{2})dx=\frac{1}{4}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left(\mu_{1}u^{4}+\mu_{2}v^{4}+2\rho u^{2}v^{2}\right)dx+\frac{3}{4}\beta\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}u^{2}vdx.

(4.21)

From
P_{b_{1},b_{2}}(u_{n},v_{n})\rightarrow 0
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla u_{n}|^{2}+|\nabla v_{n}|^{2})dx-\frac{3}{4}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left(\mu_{1}u_{n}^{4}+\mu_{2}v_{n}^{4}+2\rho u_{n}^{2}v_{n}^{2}\right)dx-\frac{3}{4}\beta\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}u_{n}^{2}v_{n}dx=o_{n}(1).

(4.22)

Together (
4.20
) with (
4.22
), we can get

\displaystyle\lambda_{1,n}b^{2}_{1}+\lambda_{2,n}b^{2}_{2}=\frac{1}{4}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left(\mu_{1}u_{n}^{4}+\mu_{2}v_{n}^{4}+2\rho u_{n}^{2}v_{n}^{2}\right)dx+\frac{3}{4}\beta\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}u_{n}^{2}v_{n}dx.

(4.23)

When
\beta>0
, it is easy to see that at least one sequence of
(\lambda_{i,n})
is positive and bounded away from 0.
Let
n\rightarrow+\infty
in (
4.23
), we have

\displaystyle\lambda_{1}b^{2}_{1}+\lambda_{2}b^{2}_{2}=\frac{1}{4}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left(\mu_{1}u^{4}+\mu_{2}v^{4}+2\rho u^{2}v^{2}\right)dx+\frac{3}{4}\beta\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}u^{2}vdx.

(4.24)

We claim that if
\lambda_{1}>0
(resp.
\lambda_{2}>0)
, then
\lambda_{2}>0
(resp.
\lambda_{1}>0
). Indeed, we know that at least one sequence of
(\lambda_{i})
is positive and bounded away from 0. If
\lambda_{2}>0
, now we argue by contradiction and assume that
\lambda_{1}\leq 0
, then

-\Delta u=-\lambda_{1}u+\beta uv+\mu_{1}u^{3}+\rho v^{2}u\geq 0.

Using a Liouville type theorem[
[
22
]
, Lemma A.2], we can deduce that
u=0
. So,
v
satisfies that

\begin{cases}-\Delta v+\lambda_{2}v=\mu_{2}v^{3},&\text{in}\ \ \mathbb{R}^{3},\\
v>0,&\text{in}\ \ \mathbb{R}^{3},\\
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}v^{2}dx=b^{2}_{2},&\text{in}\ \ \mathbb{R}^{3}.\\
\end{cases}

Therefore

\displaystyle m^{+}_{\beta}(b_{1},b_{2})=\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}J_{\beta}(u_{n},v_{n})

\displaystyle=\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\left[\frac{1}{8}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(\mu_{1}u_{n}^{4}+\mu_{2}v_{n}^{4}+2\rho u_{n}^{2}v_{n}^{2})dx-\frac{\beta}{8}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}u_{n}^{2}v_{n}dx\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{\mu_{1}}{8}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}v^{4}dx=m^{+}_{\beta}(0,b_{2}),

in contradiction with Lemma
4.6
. Thus,
\lambda_{1}>0,\ \lambda_{2}>0.

If
\lambda_{1}>0
, now we argue by contradiction and assume that
\lambda_{2}\leq 0
, then

-\Delta v=-\lambda_{2}v\frac{\beta}{2}u^{2}+\mu_{2}v^{3}+\rho u^{2}v\geq 0.

Using a Liouville type theorem[
[
22
]
, Lemma A.2], we can deduce that
v=0
. So, by the structure of system (
1.1
), we get
u=0
, which is impossible.

It is easy to see that

\displaystyle m^{+}_{\beta}(b_{1},b_{2})=\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}J_{\beta}(u_{n},v_{n})=\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\left[\frac{1}{6}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla u_{n}|^{2}+|\nabla v_{n}|^{2})dx-\frac{\beta}{4}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}u_{n}^{2}v_{n}dx\right].

Case 1
If
u=0,v=0
, by compact Sobolev embedding,

\displaystyle m^{+}_{\beta}(b_{1},b_{2})=\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}J_{\beta}(u_{n},v_{n})\geq\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\frac{1}{6}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla u_{n}|^{2}+|\nabla v_{n}|^{2})dx\geq 0.

However, from Lemma
4.4
, we know that
m^{+}_{\beta}(b_{1},b_{2})<0
, which is a contraction.

Case 2
If
u\neq 0,v=0
, indeed, if
v=0
, by the structure of system (
1.1
), we get
u=0
, so Case 2 doesn’t happen.

Case 3
If
u=0,\ v\neq 0
, we have

\displaystyle m^{+}_{\beta}(b_{1},b_{2})=\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}J_{\beta}(u_{n},v_{n})\geq\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\frac{1}{6}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla u_{n}|^{2}+|\nabla v_{n}|^{2})dx\geq 0,

contradicts with
m^{+}_{\beta}(b_{1},b_{2})<0
.

Case 4
if
u\neq 0,v\neq 0
, let

\widetilde{u}_{n}=u_{n}-u,\ \widetilde{v}_{n}=v_{n}-v,

from
[
17
, Lemma 2.4]
, we get

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\widetilde{u}_{n}|^{2}|\widetilde{v}_{n}|^{2}dx=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|u_{n}|^{2}|v_{n}|^{2}dx-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|u|^{2}|v|^{2}dx+o(1).

(4.25)

By Brezis Lieb Lemma in
[
47
]
, we can obtain that

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\widetilde{u}_{n}|^{2}\widetilde{v}_{n}dx=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|u_{n}|^{2}v_{n}dx-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|u|^{2}vdx+o(1).

(4.26)

So, from (
4.25
) and (
4.26
), we have

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=P_{b_{1},b_{2}}(u_{n},v_{n})+o(1)=P_{b_{1},b_{2}}(\widetilde{u}_{n},\widetilde{v}_{n})+P_{b_{1},b_{2}}(u,v)+o(1)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla\widetilde{u}_{n}|^{2}+|\nabla\widetilde{v}_{n}|^{2})dx-\frac{3}{4}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left(\mu_{1}\widetilde{u}_{n}^{4}+\mu_{2}\widetilde{v}_{n}^{4}+2\rho\widetilde{u}_{n}^{2}\widetilde{v}_{n}^{2}\right)dx-\frac{3}{4}\beta\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\widetilde{u}_{n}^{2}\widetilde{v}_{n}dx+o(1).

It is easy to see that
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}u^{2}\leq b^{2}_{1},\ \int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}v^{2}\leq b^{2}_{2}.
From (
4.21
) and (
4.24
), we have

\lambda_{1}\left(b^{2}_{1}-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}u^{2}dx\right)+\lambda_{2}\left(b^{2}_{2}-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}v^{2}dx\right)=0,

so

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}u^{2}=b^{2}_{1},\ \int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}v^{2}=b^{2}_{2}.

(4.27)

Therefore, we know that

\displaystyle m^{+}_{\beta}(b_{1},b_{2})

\displaystyle=\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}J_{\beta}(u_{n},v_{n})=\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}J_{\beta}(\widetilde{u}_{n},\widetilde{v}_{n})+J_{\beta}(u,v)

\displaystyle=\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\left[\frac{1}{6}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla\widetilde{u}_{n}|^{2}+|\nabla\widetilde{v}_{n}|^{2})dx-\frac{\beta}{4}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\widetilde{u}_{n}^{2}\widetilde{v}_{n}dx\right]+J_{\beta}(u,v)

\displaystyle\geq\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\bigg[\frac{1}{6}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla\widetilde{u}_{n}|^{2}+|\nabla\widetilde{v}_{n}|^{2})dx

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{|\beta|}{4}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\widetilde{u}_{n}|^{3}dx\right)^{\frac{2}{3}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\widetilde{v}_{n}|^{3}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}\bigg]+J_{\beta}(u,v)

\displaystyle\geq\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\frac{1}{6}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla\widetilde{u}_{n}|^{2}+|\nabla\widetilde{v}_{n}|^{2})dx+J_{\beta}(u,v)\geq m^{+}_{\beta}(b_{1},b_{2}).

Thus,
J_{\beta}(u,v)=m^{+}_{\beta}(b_{1},b_{2})\ \text{and}\ (u_{n},v_{n})\rightarrow(u,v)
in
H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\times H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}).

∎

4.2
Existence of a second critical point of mountain pass type

Next, we prove the existence of second critical point of mountain pass type for
J_{\beta}(u,v)\mid_{\mathrm{T}_{b_{1}}\times\mathrm{T}_{b_{2}}}
.

Lemma 4.9
.

Suppose that
J_{\beta}(u,v)<m^{+}_{\beta}(b_{1},b_{2})
. Then the value
t_{u,v}
defined by Lemma
4.4
is negative.

Proof.

From Lemma
4.4
, we know that the function
\Psi_{u,v}(t)
has exactly two critical points
s_{u,v}<t_{u,v}\in\mathbb{R}
and two zeros
c_{u,v}<d_{u,v}\in\mathbb{R}
with
s_{u,v}<c_{u,v}<t_{u,v}<d_{u,v}
. If
d_{u,v}\leq 0
, then
t_{u,v}<0.
Assume by contradiction that
d_{u,v}>0
, if
0\in(c_{u,v},t_{u,v})
, then
J_{\beta}(u,v)=\Psi_{u,v}(0)>0
contract with
J_{\beta}(u,v)<m^{+}_{\beta}(b_{1},b_{2})<0
. Therefore,
c_{u,v}>0.
By Lemma
4.4
-(2), we know that

\displaystyle m^{+}_{\beta}(b_{1},b_{2})>J_{\beta}(u,v)=\Psi_{u,v}(0)\geq\inf_{t\in(-\infty,c_{u,v}]}\Psi_{u,v}(t)\geq J_{\beta}(u,v)(s_{u,v}\star(u,v))

\displaystyle=\min\left\{J_{\beta}(t\star(u,v):t\in\mathbb{R}\ \text{and}\ (\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla(t\star u)|^{2}+|\nabla(t\star v)|^{2})dx)^{\frac{1}{2}}<R_{0}\right\}

\displaystyle=J_{\beta}(s_{u,v}\star(u,v)\geq m^{+}_{\beta}(b_{1},b_{2}),

which is a contradiction.
∎

Lemma 4.10
.

We have

m^{-}_{\beta}(b_{1},b_{2}):=\inf_{(u,v)\in\mathcal{P}^{-}_{b_{1},b_{2}}}J_{\beta}(u,v)>0.

Proof.

From Lemma
4.2
, there exists
0<R_{0}<R_{1}
such that
h(R_{0})=h(R_{1})=0
,
h(t)>0
if and only if
t\in(R_{0},R_{1})
. Let
t_{max}
be the maximum point of the function
h(t)
at the positive level
(R_{0},R_{1})
. For every
(u,v)\in\mathcal{P}^{-}_{b_{1},b_{2}}
, there exists
\tau_{u,v}\in\mathbb{R}
such that

\big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla(\tau_{u,v}\star u)|^{2}+|\nabla(\tau_{u,v}\star v)|^{2})dx\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}=t_{max},

and from Lemma
4.4
we know that
t_{u,v}\star(u,v)\in\mathcal{P}^{-}_{b_{1},b_{2}}
then
t=t_{u,v},
which implies that 0 is the unique strict maximum of
\Psi_{u,v}(t)
. Therefore,

\displaystyle J_{\beta}(u,v)

\displaystyle=\Psi_{u,v}(0)\geq\Psi_{u,v}(\tau_{u,v})=J_{\beta}(\tau_{u,v}\star(u,v))

\displaystyle\geq h\Big(\big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla(\tau_{u,v}\star u)|^{2}+|\nabla(\tau_{u,v}\star v)|^{2})dx\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big)=h(t_{max})>0,

so for any
(u,v)\in\mathcal{P}^{-}_{b_{1},b_{2}}
, we have

m^{-}_{\beta}(b_{1},b_{2}):=\inf_{(u,v)\in\mathcal{P}^{-}_{b_{1},b_{2}}}J_{\beta}(u,v)>0.

∎

Next, we use the idea introduced by Li and Zou
[
28
]
to give the energy estimate.
We observe that
w_{\lambda,\mu}=\Big(\frac{\|w\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}}{\mu b^{2}}\Big)^{2}\frac{\|w\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}^{2}}{\mu^{\frac{3}{2}}b^{2}}w\Big(\big(\frac{\|w\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}}{\mu b^{2}}\big)x\Big)
is a solution of the problem

\begin{cases}-\Delta u+\lambda u=\mu u^{3}&\text{in}\ \ \mathbb{R}^{3},\\
u>0\ \ \ &\text{in}\ \ \mathbb{R}^{3},\\
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}u^{2}dx=b^{2},\end{cases}

(4.28)

where
w
is the unique positive solution of
-\Delta u+u=u^{3}\ \text{in}\ \ \mathbb{R}^{3}
.

Lemma 4.11
.

For any
b_{1},b_{2}>0,

\rho>0,\beta>0
, we have

m^{-}_{\beta}(b_{1},b_{2})<\min\{m^{-}_{\beta}(0,b_{2}),m^{-}_{\beta}(b_{1},0)\}.

Proof.

Let
(v,\lambda_{0})\in\mathrm{T}_{b_{2}}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}
be the unique solution of equation (
4.28
) with parameters
\mu_{2}
and
b_{2}
. Let
u(x)=c\frac{\varphi(x)}{|x|^{m}},\ m<\frac{1}{2}

\varphi(x)\in C^{\infty}_{0}(B_{2}(0)),\ 0\leq\varphi(x)\leq 1,\ \varphi(x)=1\ \text{in}\ B_{1}(0).

Then
u\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
and
u\in\mathrm{T}_{b_{1}}
for some suitable c. Therefore,
(t\star u,v)\in\mathrm{T}_{b_{1}}\times\mathrm{T}_{b_{2}}
for any
t\in\mathbb{R}
. So,

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|t\star u|^{2}v^{2}dx=C_{0}e^{(3-2m)t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{\varphi^{2}(e^{t}(x))}{|x|^{2m}}v^{2}(x)dx,

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|t\star u|^{2}vdx=C_{0}e^{(3-2m)t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{\varphi^{2}(e^{t}(x))}{|x|^{2m}}v(x)dx.

From the decay properties of
[
32
]
that
v
decays exponentially

v(x)=O(|x|^{-\frac{1}{2}}e^{-\lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}_{0}|x|})\ \text{and}\ |u(x)|\leq M\ \text{in}\ \mathbb{R}^{3}.

Thus,

0<\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{v^{2}(x)}{|x|^{2m}}dx\leq C\Big(\int_{B_{R}(0)}\frac{1}{|x|^{2m}}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\setminus B_{R}(0)}|x|^{-1-2m}e^{-\lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}_{0}2|x|}\Big)<+\infty,

0<\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{v(x)}{|x|^{2m}}dx\leq C\Big(\int_{B_{R}(0)}\frac{1}{|x|^{2m}}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\setminus B_{R}(0)}|x|^{-\frac{1}{2}-2m}e^{-\lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}_{0}|x|}\Big)<+\infty.

By the Dominated Convergence Theorem, we obtain that

\lim_{t\rightarrow-\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{\varphi^{2}(e^{t}(x))}{|x|^{2m}}v^{2}(x)dx=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{v^{2}(x)}{|x|^{2m}}dx=C_{1}\in(0,+\infty),

\lim_{t\rightarrow-\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{\varphi^{2}(e^{t}(x))}{|x|^{2m}}v(x)dx=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{v(x)}{|x|^{2m}}dx=C_{2}\in(0,+\infty),

so

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|t\star u|^{2}v^{2}dx=C_{0}e^{(3-2m)t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{\varphi^{2}(e^{t}(x))}{|x|^{2m}}v^{2}(x)dx

\displaystyle=C_{0}e^{(3-2m)t}(C_{1}+o(1))\geq\frac{C_{0}C_{1}}{2}e^{(3-2m)t},

(4.29)

as
t\rightarrow-\infty
.

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|t\star u|^{2}vdx

\displaystyle=C_{0}e^{(3-2m)t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{\varphi^{2}(e^{t}(x))}{|x|^{2m}}v(x)dx

(4.30)

\displaystyle=C_{0}e^{(3-2m)t}(C_{2}+o(1))\geq\frac{C_{0}C_{2}}{2}e^{(3-2m)t}\ \text{as}\ t\rightarrow-\infty.

From Lemma
4.4
, we know that
t_{\ast}=t_{(t\star u,v)}
is a local maximum of
\Psi_{u,v}(t)
and
t_{(t\star u,v)}\star(t\star u,v)\in\mathcal{P}^{-}_{b_{1},b_{2}}
, so

\displaystyle 0=P(t_{\star}\star(t\star u,v))

\displaystyle=e^{2(t+t_{\star})}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\nabla u|^{2}dx+e^{2t_{\star}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\nabla v|^{2}dx-\frac{3e^{3(t+t_{\star})}}{4}\mu_{1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}u^{4}dx

\displaystyle-\frac{3e^{3t_{\star}}}{4}\mu_{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}v^{4}dx-\frac{3e^{3t_{\star}}}{2}\rho\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|t\star u|^{2}v^{2}dx-\frac{3\beta}{4}e^{\frac{3t_{\star}}{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|t\star u|^{2}vdx.

From (
4.29
), (
4.30
), (
4.2
) and let
t\rightarrow-\infty
, we have

e^{2t_{\star}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\nabla v|^{2}dx=\frac{3e^{3t_{\star}}}{4}\mu_{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}v^{4}dx.

Since
(v,\lambda_{0})\in\mathrm{T}_{b_{2}}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}
be the unique solution of equation (
4.28
) with parameters
\mu_{2}
and
b_{2}
, by the Pohozaev identity for equation (
4.28
) with parameters
\mu_{2}
and
b_{2}
, we can get
e^{3t_{\star}}\rightarrow 1
as
t\rightarrow-\infty.
Therefore, from Lemma 2.1 in
[
28
]
, we have

e^{2t_{\star}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\nabla v|^{2}dx-\frac{3e^{3t_{\star}}}{4}\mu_{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}v^{4}dx<m_{\beta}(0,b_{2}),

therefore

\displaystyle m^{-}_{\beta}(b_{1},b_{2})

\displaystyle\leq J_{\beta}(t_{\star}\star(t\star u,v))

\displaystyle=\frac{e^{2(t+t_{\star})}}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\nabla u|^{2}dx+\frac{e^{2t_{\star}}}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\nabla v|^{2}dx-\frac{e^{3(t+t_{\star})}}{4}\mu_{1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}u^{4}dx

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{e^{3t_{\star}}}{4}\mu_{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}v^{4}dx-\frac{e^{3t_{\star}}}{2}\rho\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|t\star u|^{2}v^{2}dx-\frac{\beta}{2}e^{\frac{3t_{\star}}{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|t\star u|^{2}vdx

\displaystyle=\frac{e^{2t_{\star}}}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\nabla v|^{2}dx-\frac{e^{3t_{\star}}}{4}\mu_{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}v^{4}dx-\frac{C_{0}C_{1}}{2}e^{(3-2m)t}-\beta\frac{C_{0}C_{2}}{2}e^{(3-2m)t}

\displaystyle<m^{-}_{\beta}(0,b_{2})-\frac{C_{0}C_{1}}{2}e^{(3-2m)t}-\beta\frac{C_{0}C_{2}}{2}e^{(3-2m)t},

from which, we see that for sufficiently small
t\ll-1
, there holds
m^{-}_{\beta}(b_{1},b_{2})<m^{-}_{\beta}(0,b_{2}).
Similarly, we can deduce that

m^{-}_{\beta}(b_{1},b_{2})<m^{-}_{\beta}(b_{1},0).

∎

Lemma 4.12
.

There is a radial symmetric Palais-Smale sequence of
J|_{\mathrm{T}_{b_{1}}\times\mathrm{T}_{b_{2}}}
at level
m^{-}_{\beta}(b_{1},b_{2})
with the additional properties
P(u_{n},v_{n})\rightarrow 0
and
u^{-}_{n},v^{-}_{n}\rightarrow 0
a.e. in
\mathbb{R}^{3}.
Then up to a subsequence
(u_{n},v_{n})\rightarrow(u,v)
in
H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\times H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
, where
(u,v)
is a positive solution of (
1.1
) for some
\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}>0.

Proof.

From (
4.2
), we define the functional
\widetilde{J}:\mathbb{R}\times H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\times H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
as following

\displaystyle\widetilde{J}_{\beta}(t,u,v)=J_{\beta}(t\star(u,v))

\displaystyle=\frac{e^{2t}}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla u|^{2}+|\nabla v|^{2})dx-\frac{\beta}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{\frac{3t}{2}}u^{2}vdx

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{e^{3t}}{4}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left(\mu_{1}u^{4}+\mu_{2}v^{4}+2\rho u^{2}v^{2}\right)dx.

It is easy to see that
\widetilde{J}
is of class
C^{1}
. Denoting
J_{\beta}^{c}=\{(u,v)\in\mathrm{T}_{b_{1}}\times\mathrm{T}_{b_{2}}:J_{\beta}(u,v)\leq c\}
and introduce the minimax class

\displaystyle\Gamma=\big\{\gamma=(\alpha,\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2})\in C([0,1],\mathbb{R}\times\mathrm{T}_{b_{1},r}\times\mathrm{T}_{b_{2},r}):\gamma(0)\in\{0\}\times\mathcal{P}^{+}_{b_{1},b_{2}},\gamma(1)\in\{0\}\times J_{\beta}^{2m(b_{1},b_{2})}\big\},

(4.31)

with associated minimax level

\sigma_{\beta}(b_{1},b_{2})=\inf_{\gamma\in\Gamma}\max_{t\in[0,1]}\widetilde{J}_{\beta}(\gamma(t)).

Let
(u,v)\in\mathrm{T}_{b_{1},r}\times\mathrm{T}_{b_{2},r}
, it is easy to see

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\big(|\nabla(t\star u)|^{2}+|\nabla(t\star v)|^{2}\big)dx\rightarrow 0\ \text{as}\ t\rightarrow-\infty,

J_{\beta}(t\star u,t\star v)\rightarrow-\infty\ \text{as}\ t\rightarrow+\infty.

From Lemma
4.4
, there exists
t_{0}\ll-1
and
t_{1}\gg 1
such that

t_{0}\star(u,v)\in\mathcal{P}^{+}_{b_{1},b_{2}},\ \ t_{1}\star(u,v)\in J_{\beta}^{2m_{\beta}(b_{1},b_{2})}

and

\gamma_{u,v}(t):t\in[0,1]\mapsto(0,((1-t)t_{0})+tt_{1})\star(u,v))\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathrm{T}_{b_{1},r}\times\mathrm{T}_{b_{2},r}

(4.32)

is a path in
\Gamma
. Thus,
\sigma(b_{1},b_{2})
is a real number. Next, we prove that for every

\gamma=(\alpha,\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2})\in\Gamma\ \text{there exists}\ t_{\gamma}\in(0,1)\ \text{such that}\ \alpha(t_{\gamma})\star(\varphi_{1}(t_{\gamma}),\varphi_{2}(t_{\gamma}))\in\mathcal{P}^{-}_{b_{1},b_{2}}.

(4.33)

Indeed,
\gamma(0)=(0,\varphi_{1}(0),\varphi_{2}(0))\in\{0\}\times\mathcal{P}^{+}_{b_{1},b_{2}}
, by Lemma
4.4
and the fact that
t\star(u,v)\in\mathcal{P}_{b_{1},b_{2}}
if and only if
(\Psi_{u,v})^{\prime}(t)=0
, we have

t_{\alpha(0)\star(\varphi_{1}(0),\varphi_{2}(0))}=t_{(\varphi_{1}(0),\varphi_{2}(0))}>s_{(\varphi_{1}(0),\varphi_{2}(0))}=0.

Since
J_{\beta}(\alpha(1)\star(\varphi_{1}(1),\varphi_{2}(1)))=\widetilde{J}_{\beta}(\gamma(1))\leq 2m_{\beta}(b_{1},b_{2})
, from Lemma
4.9
, we have that

t_{(\varphi_{1}(1),\varphi_{2}(1))}<0.

From Lemma
4.4
, the map
t_{\alpha(t)\star(\varphi_{1}(t),\varphi_{2}(t))}
is continuous in
t,
so there exists

t_{\gamma}\in(0,1)
such that
t_{\alpha(t_{\gamma})\star(\varphi_{1}(t_{\gamma}),\varphi_{2}(t_{\gamma}))}=0
, so
\text{for every }\ \gamma=(\alpha,\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2})\in\Gamma\ \text{there exists}\ t_{\gamma}\in(0,1)\ \text{such that}\ \alpha(t_{\gamma})\star(\varphi_{1}(t_{\gamma}),\varphi_{2}(t_{\gamma}))\in\mathcal{P}^{-}_{b_{1},b_{2}}.
This implies that

\max_{\gamma([0,1])}\widetilde{J}_{\beta}\geq\widetilde{J}_{\beta}(\gamma(t_{\gamma}))=J_{\beta}(\alpha(t_{\gamma})\star(\varphi_{1}(t_{\gamma}),\varphi_{2}(t_{\gamma})\geq\inf_{\mathcal{P}^{-}_{b_{1},b_{2}}\cap\mathrm{T}_{b_{1},r}\times\mathrm{T}_{b_{2},r}}J_{\beta}.

So

\sigma_{\beta}(b_{1},b_{2})\geq\inf_{\mathcal{P}^{-}_{b_{1},b_{2}}\cap\mathrm{T}_{b_{1},r}\times\mathrm{T}_{b_{2},r}}J_{\beta}.

On the other hand, if
(u,v)\in\mathcal{P}^{-}_{b_{1},b_{2}}\cap\mathrm{T}_{b_{1},r}\times\mathrm{T}_{b_{2},r}
, then
\gamma_{u,v}
defined in (
4.32
) is a path in
\Gamma
with

J_{\beta}(u,v)=\widetilde{J}_{\beta}(t,u,v)=\max_{\gamma_{u,v}([0,1])}\widetilde{J}_{\beta}\geq\sigma_{\beta}(b_{1},b_{2}),

so

\inf_{\mathcal{P}^{-}_{b_{1},b_{2}}\cap\mathrm{T}_{b_{1},r}\times\mathrm{T}_{b_{2},r}}J_{\beta}\geq\sigma_{\beta}(b_{1},b_{2}).

Thus,

\inf_{\mathcal{P}^{-}_{b_{1},b_{2}}\cap\mathrm{T}_{b_{1},r}\times\mathrm{T}_{b_{2},r}}J_{\beta}=\sigma_{\beta}(b_{1},b_{2}).

From Lemma
4.10
, we know that

\displaystyle\sigma_{\beta}(b_{1},b_{2})

\displaystyle=\inf_{\mathcal{P}^{-}_{b_{1},b_{2}}\cap\mathrm{T}_{b_{1},r}\times\mathrm{T}_{b_{2},r}}J_{\beta}>0\geq\sup_{\mathcal{P}^{+}_{b_{1},b_{2}}\cup J_{\beta}^{2m(b_{1},b_{2})}\cap\mathrm{T}_{b_{1},r}\times\mathrm{T}_{b_{2},r}}J_{\beta}

(4.34)

\displaystyle=\sup_{\{0\}\times\mathcal{P}^{+}_{b_{1},b_{2}}\cup\{0\}\times J^{2m(b_{1},b_{2})}\cap\mathrm{T}_{b_{1},r}\times\mathrm{T}_{b_{2},r}}\widetilde{J}_{\beta}.

Let
\mathcal{F}=\{\gamma([0,1]):\gamma\in\Gamma\}
. By the terminology in [
[
15
]
, section 5], which implies that
\{\gamma([0,1]):\gamma\in\Gamma\}
is a homotopy stable family of compact subset of
\mathbb{R}\times\mathrm{T}_{b_{1},r}\times\mathrm{T}_{b_{2},r}
with extended closed boundary
\{0\}\cup\times\mathcal{P}^{+}_{b_{1},b_{2}}\times\{0\}\times J^{2m(b_{1},b_{2})}_{\beta}
, and the superlevel set
\{\widetilde{J}_{\beta}\geq\sigma\}
is a dual set for
\mathcal{F}
, which implies that the assumption in [
[
15
]
, Theorem 5.2] are satisfied. Therefore, taking any minimizing sequence
\{\gamma_{n}([0,1]),\gamma_{n}=(\alpha_{n},\varphi_{1,n},\varphi_{2,n})\}
for
\sigma
with the property that
\alpha(t)=0,\ \varphi_{1,n}\geq 0,\ \varphi_{2,n}\geq 0
for every
t\in[0,1]
, there exists a sequence
(t_{n},u_{n},v_{n})\subset\mathbb{R}\times\mathrm{T}_{b_{1},r}\times\mathrm{T}_{b_{2},r}
such that
\widetilde{J}_{\beta}(t_{n},u_{n},v_{n})\rightarrow\sigma(b_{1},b_{2})
and

\partial_{t}\widetilde{J}_{\beta}(t_{n},u_{n},v_{n})\rightarrow 0,\ \|\partial_{(u,v)}\widetilde{J}_{\beta}(t_{n},u_{n},v_{n})\|_{T_{u_{n}\mathrm{T}_{b_{1},r}}\times T_{v_{n}\mathrm{T}_{b_{2},r}}}\rightarrow 0

(4.35)

|t_{n}|+dist((u_{n},v_{n}),(\varphi_{1,n}([0,1]),\varphi_{2,n}([0,1])))\rightarrow 0.

(4.36)

Let
(\widetilde{u}_{n},\widetilde{v}_{n})=t_{n}\star(u_{n},v_{n})\in\mathrm{T}_{b_{1},r}\times\mathrm{T}_{b_{2},r}
. By (
4.36
), we know that
t_{n}
is bounded and
\widetilde{u}^{-}_{n},\widetilde{v}^{-}_{n}\rightarrow 0
a.e. in
\mathbb{R}^{3}
. From (
4.35
), we can get

P(\widetilde{u}_{n},\widetilde{v}_{n})=\partial_{t}\widetilde{J}_{\beta}(t_{n},u_{n},v_{n})\rightarrow 0,

and

\displaystyle J_{\beta}^{\prime}(\widetilde{u}_{n},\widetilde{v}_{n})[\phi,\psi]=\partial_{(u,v)}\widetilde{J}_{\beta}(t_{n},u_{n},v_{n})[(-t_{n})\star(\phi,\psi)]

\displaystyle=o(1)\|(-t_{n})\star(\phi,\psi)\|_{H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\times H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle=o(1)\|(\phi,\psi)\|_{H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\times H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})},

therefore
(\widetilde{u}_{n},\widetilde{v}_{n})
is a radial Palais-Smale sequence of
\widetilde{J}_{\beta}|_{\mathrm{T}_{b_{1},r}\times\mathrm{T}_{b_{2},r}}
and hence a radial symmetric Palais-Smale sequence of
\widetilde{J}_{\beta}|_{\mathrm{T}_{b_{1}}\times\mathrm{T}_{b_{2}}}
at level
\sigma_{\beta}(b_{1},b_{2}).

By the same arguments as Lemma
4.8
, we can prove prove the
H^{1}
convergence of the Palais-Smale sequence. We should point out that there are several differences form the proof of Lemma

4.8
. First, to eliminate the disappearance of the solutions, we use
m^{-}(b_{1},b_{2})>0
to get a contradiction. To eliminate the semi-trivial solution, we use Brézis-Lieb lemma and Lemma
4.11
to get a contradiction.

Hence,

\displaystyle m^{-}_{\beta}(b_{1},b_{2})

\displaystyle=\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}J_{\beta}(\widetilde{u}_{n},\widetilde{v}_{n})=\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}J_{\beta}(w_{n},\sigma_{n})+J_{\beta}(u,v)

\displaystyle=\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\left[\frac{1}{6}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla w_{n}|^{2}+|\nabla\sigma_{n}|^{2})dx-\frac{\beta}{4}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}w_{n}^{2}\sigma_{n}dx\right]+J_{\beta}(u,v)

\displaystyle\geq\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\bigg[\frac{1}{6}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla w_{n}|^{2}+|\nabla\sigma_{n}|^{2})dx-\frac{|\beta|}{4}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|w_{n}|^{3}dx\right)^{\frac{2}{3}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\sigma_{n}|^{3}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}\bigg]+J_{\beta}(u,v)

\displaystyle\geq\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\frac{1}{6}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla w_{n}|^{2}+|\nabla\sigma_{n}|^{2})dx+J_{\beta}(u,v)\geq m^{-}_{\beta}(b_{1},b_{2}).

Thus,
J_{\beta}(u,v)=m^{-}_{\beta}(b_{1},b_{2})\ \text{and}\ (\widetilde{u}_{n},\widetilde{v}_{n})\rightarrow(u,v)
in
H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\times H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}).

∎

Lemma 4.13
.

We have

m^{-}_{\beta}(b_{1},b_{2})=\inf_{(u,v)\in\mathrm{T}_{b_{1},r}\times\mathrm{T}_{b_{2},r}}\max_{t\in\mathbb{R}}J_{\beta}(t\star(u,v)).

Proof.

On the one hand

m^{-}_{\beta}(b_{1},b_{2})=\inf_{(u,v)\in\mathcal{P}^{-}_{b_{1},b_{2}}}J_{\beta}(u,v)=J_{\beta}(\widetilde{u},\widetilde{v}).

Then, by Lemma
4.4
, we have

m^{-}_{\beta}(b_{1},b_{2})=J_{\beta}(\widetilde{u},\widetilde{v})=\max_{t\in\mathbb{R}}J_{\beta}(t\star(\widetilde{u},\widetilde{v}))\geq\inf_{(u,v)\in\mathrm{T}_{b_{1},r}\times\mathrm{T}_{b_{2},r}}\max_{t\in\mathbb{R}}J_{\beta}(t\star(u,v)).

On the other hand, for any
(u,v)\in\mathrm{T}_{b_{1},r}\times\mathrm{T}_{b_{2},r},
we have
t_{u,v}\star(u,v)\in\mathcal{P}^{-}_{b_{1},b_{2}}
and hence

\max_{t\in\mathbb{R}}J_{\beta}(t\star(u,v))=J_{\beta}(t_{u,v}\star(u,v))\geq m^{-}_{\beta}(b_{1},b_{2}).

∎

Before giving the estimate of
m^{-}_{\beta}(b_{1},b_{2})
, we would like to study the dependence of
m^{-}_{\beta}(b_{1},b_{2})
on
\beta
.

Lemma 4.14
.

For any
0<\beta_{1}<\beta_{2}
, then
m^{-}_{\beta_{2}}(b_{1},b_{2})\leq m^{-}_{\beta_{1}}(b_{1},b_{2})\leq m^{-}_{0}(b_{1},b_{2}).

Proof.

By Lemma
4.13

\displaystyle m^{-}_{\beta_{2}}(b_{1},b_{2})\leq\max_{t\in\mathbb{R}}J_{\beta_{2}}(t\star(\widetilde{u}_{\beta_{1}},\widetilde{v}_{\beta_{1}}))

\displaystyle\leq\max_{t\in\mathbb{R}}J_{\beta_{1}}(t\star(\widetilde{u}_{\beta_{1}},\widetilde{v}_{\beta_{1}}))

\displaystyle=J_{\beta_{1}}(\widetilde{u}_{\beta_{1}},\widetilde{v}_{\beta_{1}})=m^{-}_{\beta_{1}}(b_{1},b_{2}),

m^{-}_{\beta_{1}}(b_{1},b_{2})\leq\max_{t\in\mathbb{R}}J_{\beta_{1}}(t\star(\widetilde{u}_{0},\widetilde{v}_{0}))\leq\max_{t\in\mathbb{R}}J_{0}(t\star(\widetilde{u}_{0},\widetilde{v}_{0}))=J_{0}(\widetilde{u}_{0},\widetilde{v}_{0})=m^{-}_{0}(b_{1},b_{2}).

∎

Proof of Theorem
1.3
.

Let us consider a minimizing sequence
(u_{n},v_{n})
for
J_{\beta}(u,v)\mid_{A_{R_{0}}}
, we assume that
(u_{n},v_{n})\in\mathrm{T}_{b_{1}}\times\mathrm{T}_{b_{2}}
is radial decreasing for every
n
. Furthermore, by Lemma
4.4
, for every
n
we can take
s_{u_{n},v_{n}}\ast(u_{n},v_{n})\in\mathcal{P}^{+}_{b_{1},b_{2}}
such that
(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla(s_{u_{n},v_{n}}\star u_{n})|^{2}+|\nabla(s_{u_{n},v_{n}}\star v_{n})|^{2})dx)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq R_{0}
and

\displaystyle J_{\beta}(s_{u,v}\star(u_{n},v_{n}))

\displaystyle=\min\bigg\{J_{\beta}(t\star(u_{n},v_{n}):t\in\mathbb{R}\ \text{and}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla(t\star u_{n})|^{2}+|\nabla(t\star v_{n})|^{2})dx\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}<R_{0}\bigg\}

\displaystyle<J_{\beta}(u_{n},v_{n}).

Thus, we obtain a new minimizing sequence
\{w_{n},\sigma_{n}\}=\{s_{u_{n},u_{n}}\star v_{n},s_{u_{n},v_{n}}\star v_{n}\}
with
(w_{n},\sigma_{n})\in\mathrm{T}_{b_{1,r}}\times\mathrm{T}_{b_{2,r}}\cap\mathcal{P}^{+}_{a,\mu}
radially decreasing for every
n
. By Lemma
4.5
, we have
\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla w_{n}|^{2}+|\nabla\sigma_{n}|^{2})dx\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq R_{0}
for every
n
and hence by Ekeland’s variational principle in a standard way, we know the existence of a new minimizing sequence for
\{u_{n},v_{n}\}\subset A_{R_{0}}
for
m_{\beta}^{+}(b_{1},b_{2})
with
\|(u_{n},v_{n})-(w_{n},\sigma_{n})\|\rightarrow 0
as
n\rightarrow+\infty
, which is also a Palais-Smale sequence for
J_{\beta}(u,v)
on
\mathrm{T}_{b_{1}}\times\mathrm{T}_{b_{2}}
. By the boundedness of
\{(w_{n},\sigma_{n})\}
,
\|(u_{n},v_{n})-(w_{n},\sigma_{n})\|\rightarrow 0
, Brézis-Lieb lemma and Sobolev embedding theorem, we have

P_{b_{1},b_{2}}(u_{n},v_{n})=P_{b_{1},b_{2}}(w_{n},\sigma_{n})+o(1)\rightarrow 0\ \ \text{and}\ u^{-}_{n},v^{-}_{n}\rightarrow 0\ a.e.\text{in}\ \mathbb{R}^{3}.

Hence
(u_{n},v_{n})
satisfies all the assumptions of Lemma
4.8
, as a consequence, up to a subsequence
(u_{n},v_{n})\rightarrow(u,v)
strongly in
H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\times H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
and
(u,v)
is an interior local minimizer for
J_{\beta}(u,v)|_{A_{R_{0}}}
.
Finally, we prove that
(u,v)
is in
\mathcal{P}^{+}_{b_{1},b_{2}}(u,v)
. i.e.
\beta\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}u^{2}vdx>0
. Indeed, from
(u_{n},v_{n})\in\mathcal{P}^{+}_{b_{1},b_{2}}(u,v)
, we have
\beta\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}u^{2}vdx\geq 0
. Assume
\beta\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}u^{2}vdx=0
, then from Pohozaev identity, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla u|^{2}+|\nabla v|^{2})dx=\frac{3}{4}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(\mu_{1}u^{4}+\mu_{2}v^{4}+2\rho u^{2}v^{2})dx.

Thus, from (
2.1
)-(
2.3
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla u|^{2}+|\nabla v|^{2})dx

\displaystyle=\frac{3}{4}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left(\mu_{1}u^{4}+\mu_{2}v^{4}+2\rho u^{2}v^{2}\right)dx

\displaystyle\leq\frac{3\mu_{1}}{4}C^{4}_{3,4}b_{1}\|\nabla u\|^{3}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\frac{3\mu_{2}}{4}C^{4}_{3,4}b_{2}\|\nabla v\|^{3}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{3}{4}\rho C^{4}_{3,4}b^{\frac{1}{2}}_{1}b^{\frac{1}{2}}_{2}\left[\|\nabla u\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\|\nabla v\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\right]^{\frac{3}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{3}{4}C^{4}_{3,4}(\mu_{1}b_{1}+\mu_{2}b_{2}+\rho b^{\frac{1}{2}}_{1}b^{\frac{1}{2}}_{2})\left[\|\nabla u\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\|\nabla v\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\right]^{\frac{3}{2}},

so,

\displaystyle\left[\|\nabla u\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\|\nabla v\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}\geq\frac{4}{3C^{4}_{3,4}(\mu_{1}b_{1}+\mu_{2}b_{2}+\rho b^{\frac{1}{2}}_{1}b^{\frac{1}{2}}_{2})}.

(4.37)

From Corollary
4.1
and the definition of
R_{0}
in Lemma
4.2
, we have

\displaystyle\left[\|\nabla u\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\|\nabla v\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}<R_{0}\leq\widetilde{t}=\frac{2}{3C^{4}_{3,4}(\mu_{1}b_{1}+\mu_{2}b_{1}+\rho b^{\frac{1}{2}}_{1}b^{\frac{1}{2}}_{2})}.

(4.38)

a contradiction. Thus
\beta\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}u^{2}vdx>0
and
(u,v)\in\mathcal{P}^{+}_{b_{1},b_{2}}(u,v)
. From Lemma
4.5
, we know that
(u,v)
is a ground normalized solution. From Lemma
4.12
, we get a second critical point of mountain pass type for
J_{\beta}(u,v)\mid_{\mathrm{T}_{b_{1}}\times\mathrm{T}_{b_{2}}}
.

Next, we prove the second part of Theorem
1.3
, that is the limit behavior of the ground state solution as
\beta\rightarrow 0.

For
b_{1},b_{2}>0
fixed, from the proof Lemma
4.2
, we can deduce that when
\beta\rightarrow 0
, then
R_{0}(b_{1},b_{2},\rho,\beta)\rightarrow 0
. By corollary
4.1
, when
(\widehat{u},\widehat{v})
is the ground normalized solution obtained in Theorem
1.3
, then

\big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla u|^{2}+|\nabla v|^{2})dx\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}<R_{0}(b_{1},b_{2},\rho,\beta)\rightarrow 0,

and

\displaystyle 0>

\displaystyle m^{+}_{\beta}(b_{1},b_{2})=J_{\beta}(u,v)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla u|^{2}+|\nabla v|^{2})dx-\mathcal{D}_{1}\big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\nabla u|^{2}dx\big)^{\frac{3}{2}}-\mathcal{D}_{2}\big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\nabla v|^{2}dx\big)^{\frac{3}{2}}

\displaystyle\quad-\rho\mathcal{D}_{3}\big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla u|^{2}+|\nabla v|^{2})dx\big)^{\frac{3}{2}}-|\beta|\mathcal{D}_{4}\big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla u|^{2}+|\nabla v|^{2})dx\big)^{\frac{3}{4}}

\displaystyle\geq h\Big(\big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla u|^{2}+|\nabla v|^{2})dx\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big)\rightarrow 0,

thus

m^{+}_{\beta}(b_{1},b_{2})\rightarrow 0.

Denote
\{(\widetilde{u}_{\beta},\widetilde{v}_{\beta}):0<\beta<\overline{\beta}\}
with
\overline{\beta}
small enough. By the same arguments as Lemma
4.8
, we can obtain the
H^{1}
convergence. First, to eliminate the disappearance of the solutions, we use Lemma
4.14
and
0<m^{-}_{\beta}(b_{1},b_{2})<m^{-}_{0}(b_{1},b_{2})
to get a contradiction. To eliminate the semi-trivial solution, we use Brézis-Lieb lemma and energy comparison to get a contradiction.

Hence,

\displaystyle m^{-}_{0}(b_{1},b_{2})

\displaystyle=\lim_{\beta\rightarrow 0}J_{\beta}(\widetilde{u}_{\beta},\widetilde{v}_{\beta})=\lim_{\beta\rightarrow 0}J_{\beta}(w_{\beta},\sigma_{\beta})+\lim_{\beta\rightarrow 0}J_{\beta}(\widetilde{u},\widetilde{v})

\displaystyle=\lim_{\beta\rightarrow 0}\left[\frac{1}{6}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla w_{\beta}|^{2}+|\nabla\sigma_{\beta}|^{2})dx-\frac{\beta}{4}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}w_{\beta}^{2}\sigma_{\beta}dx\right]+\lim_{\beta\rightarrow 0}J_{\beta}(\widetilde{u},\widetilde{v})

\displaystyle\geq\lim_{\beta\rightarrow 0}\frac{1}{6}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla w_{\beta}|^{2}+|\nabla\sigma_{\beta}|^{2})dx+\lim_{\beta\rightarrow 0}J_{\beta}(\widetilde{u},\widetilde{v})\geq m^{-}_{0}(b_{1},b_{2}).

Thus,
\lim_{\beta\rightarrow 0}J(\widetilde{u}_{\beta},\widetilde{v}_{\beta})=m^{-}_{0}(b_{1},b_{2})\ \text{and}\ (\widetilde{u}_{\beta},\widetilde{v}_{\beta})\rightarrow(\widetilde{u},\widetilde{v})
in
H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\times H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}).

∎

5
Proof of Theorem
1.4

In this section, we give a refined upper of
m^{+}_{\beta}(b_{1},b_{2})
and search for
(\lambda_{1,b_{1},b_{2}},\lambda_{2,b_{1},b_{2}},u_{b_{1},b_{2}},v_{b_{1},b_{2}})
solving

\begin{cases}-\Delta u+\lambda_{1}u=\beta uv&\text{in}\ \ \mathbb{R}^{3},\\
-\Delta v+\lambda_{2}v=\frac{\beta}{2}u^{2}&\text{in}\ \ \mathbb{R}^{3},\end{cases}

(5.1)

satisfying the additional conditions

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}u^{2}dx=b^{2}_{1}\ \text{and}\ \int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}v^{2}dx=b^{2}_{2}.

(5.2)

Denote

J_{0}(u,v)=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla u|^{2}+|\nabla v|^{2})dx-\frac{\beta}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}u^{2}vdx,

on the constraint
\mathrm{T}_{b_{1}}\times\mathrm{T}_{b_{2}}
.

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}^{0}_{b_{1},b_{2}}(u,v):=\Big\{

\displaystyle(u,v)\in\mathrm{T}_{b_{1}}\times\mathrm{T}_{b_{2}}:P^{0}_{b_{1},b_{2}}(u,v)=0\Big\},

where

P^{0}_{b_{1},b_{2}}(u,v)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla u|^{2}+|\nabla v|^{2})dx-\frac{3}{4}\beta\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}u^{2}vdx.

Then, the solution of (
5.1
)-(
5.2
) can be found as minimizers of

m^{+}_{0}(b_{1},b_{2})=\inf_{\mathrm{T}_{b_{1}}\times\mathrm{T}_{b_{2}}}J_{0}(u,v)>-\infty.

If
(u_{0},v_{0})
is the unique positive solution of (
1.12
), then
(u_{0},v_{0})=(\sqrt{2}\beta^{-1}w,\beta^{-1}w)
, and
w
is the unique positive solution of (
1.13
).

Let

\theta_{1}=\frac{2\beta^{2}b_{1}^{\frac{6}{5}}b_{2}^{\frac{4}{5}}}{16^{\frac{2}{5}}\|w\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}},\ \theta_{2}=\frac{\beta^{2}b_{1}^{\frac{8}{5}}b_{2}^{\frac{2}{5}}}{16^{\frac{1}{5}}\|w\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}},\ L_{1}=\frac{2\beta^{4}b_{1}^{\frac{14}{5}}b_{2}^{\frac{6}{5}}}{16^{\frac{3}{5}}\|w\|^{4}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}},\ \ L_{2}=\frac{4\beta^{4}b_{1}^{\frac{12}{5}}b_{2}^{\frac{8}{5}}}{16^{\frac{4}{5}}\|w\|^{4}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}},

\lambda_{1,b_{1},b_{2}}=\frac{4\beta^{4}b_{1}^{\frac{12}{5}}b_{2}^{\frac{8}{5}}}{16^{\frac{4}{5}}\|w\|^{4}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}},\ \ \ \lambda_{2,b_{1},b_{2}}=\frac{\beta^{4}b_{1}^{\frac{16}{5}}b_{2}^{\frac{4}{5}}}{16^{\frac{2}{5}}\|w\|^{4}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}},

we have following lemma.

Lemma 5.1
.

(
5.1
)-(
5.2
) has a unique positive solution

\big(\lambda_{1,b_{1},b_{2}},\lambda_{2,b_{1},b_{2}},L_{1}u_{0}(\theta_{1}x),L_{2}v_{0}(\theta_{2}x)\big).

Moreover,

\displaystyle m^{+}_{0}(b_{1},b_{2})=J_{0}(L_{1}u_{0}(\theta_{1}x),L_{2}v_{0}(\theta_{2}x))=-\frac{1}{6}\Big[\frac{4\beta^{4}b_{1}^{\frac{22}{5}}b_{2}^{\frac{8}{5}}}{16^{\frac{4}{5}}\|w\|^{6}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}}+\frac{\beta^{4}b_{1}^{\frac{16}{5}}b_{2}^{\frac{14}{5}}}{16^{\frac{2}{5}}\|w\|^{6}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}}\Big]\|\nabla w\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}.

Proof.

By elementary calculation, we have

(\lambda_{1,b_{1},b_{2}},\lambda_{2,b_{1},b_{2}},L_{1}u_{0}(\theta_{1}x),L_{2}v_{0}(\theta_{2}x))

is the unique positive solution of (
5.1
)-(
5.2
). Furthermore, we have

\displaystyle m^{+}_{0}(b_{1},b_{2})

\displaystyle=J_{0}(L_{1}u_{0}(\theta_{1}x),L_{2}v_{0}(\theta_{2}x))

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{6}\Big[\frac{L^{2}_{1}}{\theta_{1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\nabla u_{0}|^{2}dx+\frac{L^{2}_{2}}{\theta_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\nabla v_{0}|^{2}dx\Big]

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{6}\Big[2\frac{L^{2}_{1}}{\theta_{1}}\beta^{-2}+\frac{L^{2}_{2}}{\theta_{2}}\beta^{-2}\Big]\|\nabla w\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{6}\Big[\frac{4\beta^{4}b_{1}^{\frac{22}{5}}b_{2}^{\frac{8}{5}}}{16^{\frac{4}{5}}\|w\|^{6}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}}+\frac{\beta^{4}b_{1}^{\frac{16}{5}}b_{2}^{\frac{14}{5}}}{16^{\frac{2}{5}}\|w\|^{6}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}}\Big]\|\nabla w\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}.

∎

Lemma 5.2
.

We have

m^{-}_{\beta}(b_{1},b_{2})<m^{+}_{0}(b_{1},b_{2})=-\frac{1}{6}\Big[\frac{4\beta^{4}b_{1}^{\frac{22}{5}}b_{2}^{\frac{8}{5}}}{16^{\frac{4}{5}}\|w\|^{6}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}}+\frac{\beta^{4}b_{1}^{\frac{16}{5}}b_{2}^{\frac{14}{5}}}{16^{\frac{2}{5}}\|w\|^{6}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}}\Big]\|\nabla w\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}.

Proof.

Since

\displaystyle J_{0}(u,v)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla u|^{2}+|\nabla v|^{2})dx-\frac{\beta}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}u^{2}vdx

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla u|^{2}+|\nabla v|^{2})dx-\frac{|\beta|}{2}\Big[\big(\frac{2}{3}b^{\frac{3}{2}}_{1}+\frac{1}{3}b^{\frac{3}{2}}_{2}\big)C^{3}_{3,3}\big[\|\nabla u\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\|\nabla v\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\big]^{\frac{3}{4}}\Big]

\displaystyle=g\Big(\big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla u|^{2}+|\nabla v|^{2})dx\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big),

where

g(t)=\frac{1}{2}t^{2}-\frac{|\beta|}{2}\Big(\frac{2}{3}b^{\frac{3}{2}}_{1}+\frac{1}{3}b^{\frac{3}{2}}_{2}\Big)C^{3}_{3,3}t^{\frac{3}{2}}.

It is easy to see that
g(t)<0
if
t\in(0,\widetilde{t})
and
g(t)>0
if
t\in(\widetilde{t},+\infty)
, where
\widetilde{t}=\Big[|\beta|\big(\frac{2}{3}b^{\frac{3}{2}}_{1}+\frac{1}{3}b^{\frac{3}{2}}_{2}\big)C^{3}_{3,3}\Big]^{2}
.
Since
J_{0}(L_{1}u_{0}(\theta_{1}x),L_{2}v_{0}(\theta_{2}x))=m^{+}_{0}(b_{1},b_{2})<0
, we get

\Big[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla L_{1}u_{0}(\theta_{1}x)|^{2}+|\nabla L_{2}v_{0}(\theta_{2}x)|^{2})dx\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}<\widetilde{t}.

From Lemma
5.1
, the definition of
R_{0}
in Lemma
4.2
and the definition of
\widetilde{t}
, we have
\widetilde{t}<R_{0}
,

\|L_{1}u_{0}(\theta_{1}x)\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}=b^{2}_{1},\ \|L_{2}v_{0}(\theta_{2}x)\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}=b^{2}_{2},

and

\displaystyle\Big[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla L_{1}u_{0}(\theta_{1}x)|^{2}+|\nabla L_{2}v_{0}(\theta_{2}x)|^{2})dx\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}<\widetilde{t}<R_{0}.

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle m^{-}_{\beta}(b_{1},b_{2})

\displaystyle=\inf_{A_{R_{0}}}J_{\beta}(u,v)\leq J_{\beta}(L_{1}u_{0}(\theta_{1}x),L_{2}v_{0}(\theta_{2}x))

\displaystyle\quad<J_{0}(L_{1}u_{0}(\theta_{1}x),L_{2}v_{0}(\theta_{2}x))=m_{0}(b_{1},b_{2})

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{6}\Big[\frac{4\beta^{4}b_{1}^{\frac{22}{5}}b_{2}^{\frac{8}{5}}}{16^{\frac{4}{5}}\|w\|^{6}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}}+\frac{\beta^{4}b_{1}^{\frac{16}{5}}b_{2}^{\frac{14}{5}}}{16^{\frac{2}{5}}\|w\|^{6}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}}\Big]\|\nabla w\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}.

∎

Proof of Theorem
1.4
.

Let
b_{1,k},\ b_{2,k}\rightarrow 0^{+}
with
b_{1,k}\backsim b_{2,k}
as
k\rightarrow+\infty
and
(u_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}},v_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}})\in A_{R_{0}}
be a positive minimizer of
m(b_{1,k},b_{2,k},R_{0})

for each
k\in\mathbb{N}
. From

\displaystyle P_{b_{1},b_{2}}(u_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}},v_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}})

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla u_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}|^{2}+|\nabla v_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}|^{2})dx-\frac{3}{4}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left(\mu_{1}u_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}^{4}+\mu_{2}v_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}^{4}+2\rho u_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}^{2}v_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}^{2}\right)dx

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{3}{4}\beta\int_{\mathbb{R}^{N}}u_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}^{2}v_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}dx=0,

we have

\displaystyle J_{\beta}(u_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}},v_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{6}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla u_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}|^{2}+|\nabla v_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}|^{2})dx-\frac{\beta}{4}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{N}}u_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}^{2}v_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}dx

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{6}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla u_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}|^{2}+|\nabla v_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}|^{2})dx

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{4}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\big(\mu_{1}u_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}^{4}+\mu_{2}v_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}^{4}+2\rho u_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}^{2}v_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}^{2}\big)dx

\displaystyle<-\frac{1}{6}\Big[\frac{4\beta^{4}b_{1,k}^{\frac{22}{5}}b_{2,k}^{\frac{8}{5}}}{16^{\frac{4}{5}}\|w\|^{6}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}}+\frac{\beta^{4}b_{1,k}^{\frac{16}{5}}b_{2,k}^{\frac{14}{5}}}{16^{\frac{2}{5}}\|w\|^{6}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}}\Big]\|\nabla w\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}.

Thus

\displaystyle\Big[\frac{4\beta^{4}b_{1,k}^{\frac{22}{5}}b_{2,k}^{\frac{8}{5}}}{16^{\frac{4}{5}}\|w\|^{6}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}}+\frac{\beta^{4}b_{1,k}^{\frac{16}{5}}b_{2,k}^{\frac{14}{5}}}{16^{\frac{2}{5}}\|w\|^{6}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}}\Big]\|\nabla w\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla u_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}|^{2}+|\nabla v_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}|^{2})dx

and

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla u_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}|^{2}+|\nabla v_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}|^{2})dx<\left[\frac{3|\beta|}{2}\Big[\big(\frac{2}{3}b^{\frac{3}{2}}_{1,k}+\frac{1}{3}b^{\frac{3}{2}}_{2,k}\big)C^{3}_{3,3}\Big]\right]^{4}.

Therefore

\displaystyle\Big[\frac{4\beta^{4}b_{1,k}^{\frac{22}{5}}b_{2,k}^{\frac{8}{5}}}{16^{\frac{4}{5}}\|w\|^{6}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}}+\frac{\beta^{4}b_{1,k}^{\frac{16}{5}}b_{2,k}^{\frac{14}{5}}}{16^{\frac{2}{5}}\|w\|^{6}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}}\Big]\|\nabla w\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

(5.3)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla u_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}|^{2}+|\nabla v_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}|^{2})dx<\left[\frac{3|\beta|}{2}\Big[\big(\frac{2}{3}b^{\frac{3}{2}}_{1,k}+\frac{1}{3}b^{\frac{3}{2}}_{2,k}\big)C^{3}_{3,3}\Big]\right]^{4}.

When
b_{1,k}\backsim b_{2,k}
, we can get

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla u_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}|^{2}+|\nabla v_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}|^{2})dx\backsim b_{1,k}^{\frac{22}{5}}b_{2,k}^{\frac{8}{5}}+b_{1,k}^{\frac{16}{5}}b_{2,k}^{\frac{14}{5}}.

(5.4)

From (
2.1
)-(
2.2
) and (
5.3
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\big(\mu_{1}u_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}^{4}+\mu_{2}v_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}^{4}+2\rho u_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}^{2}v_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}^{2}\big)dx

(5.5)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{4}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Big((\mu_{1}+\rho)u_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}^{4}+(\mu_{2}+\rho)v_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}^{4}\Big)dx

\displaystyle\leq\Big[\frac{\mu_{1}+\rho}{4}C^{4}_{3,4}b_{1,k}+\frac{\mu_{2}+\rho}{4}C^{4}_{3,4}b_{2,k}\Big]\left[\|\nabla u_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\|\nabla v_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\right]^{\frac{3}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\Big[\frac{\mu_{1}+\rho}{4}C^{4}_{3,4}b_{1,k}+\frac{\mu_{2}+\rho}{4}C^{4}_{3,4}b_{2,k}\Big]\left[\frac{3|\beta|}{2}\Big[\big(\frac{2}{3}b^{\frac{3}{2}}_{1,k}+\frac{1}{3}b^{\frac{3}{2}}_{2,k}\big)C^{3}_{3,3}\Big]\right]^{6}\rightarrow 0

as
k\rightarrow+\infty.

Since

m^{-}_{\beta}(b_{1,k},b_{2,k})<-\frac{1}{6}\Big[\frac{4\beta^{4}b_{1,k}^{\frac{22}{5}}b_{2,k}^{\frac{8}{5}}}{16^{\frac{4}{5}}\|w\|^{6}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}}+\frac{\beta^{4}b_{1,k}^{\frac{16}{5}}b_{2,k}^{\frac{14}{5}}}{16^{\frac{2}{5}}\|w\|^{6}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}}\Big]\|\nabla w\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})},

we obtain

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{6}\Big[\frac{4\beta^{4}b_{1,k}^{\frac{22}{5}}b_{2,k}^{\frac{8}{5}}}{16^{\frac{4}{5}}\|w\|^{6}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}}+\frac{\beta^{4}b_{1,k}^{\frac{16}{5}}b_{2,k}^{\frac{14}{5}}}{16^{\frac{2}{5}}\|w\|^{6}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}}\Big]\|\nabla w\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

(5.6)

\displaystyle>m^{-}_{\beta}(b_{1,k},b_{2,k})=J_{\beta}(u_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}},v_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}})

\displaystyle\geq\inf_{\mathrm{T}_{b_{1,k}}\times\mathrm{T}_{b_{2,k}}}\Big\{\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla u|^{2}+|\nabla v|^{2})dx-\frac{\beta}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}u^{2}vdx\Big\}

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{4}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\big(\mu_{1}u_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}^{4}+\mu_{2}v_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}^{4}+2\rho u_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}^{2}v_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}^{2}\big)dx

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{6}\Big[\frac{4\beta^{4}b_{1,k}^{\frac{22}{5}}b_{2,k}^{\frac{8}{5}}}{16^{\frac{4}{5}}\|w\|^{6}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}}+\frac{\beta^{4}b_{1,k}^{\frac{16}{5}}b_{2,k}^{\frac{14}{5}}}{16^{\frac{2}{5}}\|w\|^{6}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}}\Big]\|\nabla w\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{4}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\big(\mu_{1}u_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}^{4}+\mu_{2}v_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}^{4}+2\rho u_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}^{2}v_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}^{2}\big)dx

\displaystyle\geq-\frac{1}{6}\Big[\frac{4\beta^{4}b_{1,k}^{\frac{22}{5}}b_{2,k}^{\frac{8}{5}}}{16^{\frac{4}{5}}\|w\|^{6}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}}+\frac{\beta^{4}b_{1,k}^{\frac{16}{5}}b_{2,k}^{\frac{14}{5}}}{16^{\frac{2}{5}}\|w\|^{6}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}}\Big]\|\nabla w\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

(5.7)

\displaystyle\quad-\Big[\frac{\mu_{1}+\rho}{4}C^{4}_{3,4}b_{1,k}+\frac{\mu_{2}+\rho}{4}C^{4}_{3,4}b_{2,k}\Big]\left[\frac{3|\beta|}{2}\Big[\big(\frac{2}{3}b^{\frac{3}{2}}_{1,k}+\frac{1}{3}b^{\frac{3}{2}}_{2,k}\big)C^{3}_{3,3}\Big]\right]^{6}.

When
b_{1,k}\backsim b_{2,k}
, we can get

\displaystyle m^{-}_{\beta}(b_{1,k},b_{2,k})\backsim b_{1,k}^{\frac{22}{5}}b_{2,k}^{\frac{8}{5}}+b_{1,k}^{\frac{16}{5}}b_{2,k}^{\frac{14}{5}}

(5.8)

and

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla u_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}|^{2}+|\nabla v_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}|^{2})dx-\frac{\beta}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{N}}u_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}^{2}v_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}dx\backsim b_{1,k}^{\frac{22}{5}}b_{2,k}^{\frac{8}{5}}+b_{1,k}^{\frac{16}{5}}b_{2,k}^{\frac{14}{5}}.

(5.9)

The Lagrange multipliers rule implies the existence of some
\lambda_{1,k},\lambda_{2,k}\in\mathbb{R}
such that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(\nabla u_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}\nabla\varphi)dx+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(\lambda_{1,k}u_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}\varphi)dx

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(\mu_{1}u_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}^{3}\varphi+\rho v_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}^{2}u_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}\varphi)dx+\beta\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}u_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}v_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}\varphi,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(\nabla v_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}\nabla\psi)dx+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(\lambda_{2,k}v_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}\psi)dx

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(\mu_{2}v_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}^{3}\psi+\rho u_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}^{2}v_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}\psi)dx+\frac{\beta}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}u_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}^{2}\psi,

for each
\varphi,\psi\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
. Taking
\varphi=u_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}
and
\psi=v_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}
, we have

\displaystyle\lambda_{1,k}b_{1,k}^{2}

\displaystyle=-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\nabla u_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}|^{2}dx+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(\mu_{1}u_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}^{4}+\rho v_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}^{2}u^{2}_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}})dx

(5.10)

\displaystyle+\beta\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}u^{2}_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}v_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}},

\displaystyle\lambda_{2,k}b_{2,k}^{2}

\displaystyle=-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\nabla v_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}|^{2}dx+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(\mu_{1}v_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}^{4}+\rho v_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}^{2}u^{2}_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}})dx

(5.11)

\displaystyle+\frac{\beta}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}u^{2}_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}v_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}.

Since
P_{b_{1},b_{2}}(u_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}},v_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}})=0,

we get

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla u_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}|^{2}+|\nabla v_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}|^{2})dx-\frac{3}{4}\beta\int_{\mathbb{R}^{N}}u_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}^{2}v_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}dx

(5.12)

\displaystyle-\frac{3}{4}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left(\mu_{1}u_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}^{4}+\mu_{2}v_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}^{4}+2\rho u_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}^{2}v_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}^{2}\right)dx=0.

Hence, from (
5.10
), (
5.11
), (
5.12
) and
b_{1,k}\backsim b_{2,k}
, we have

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla u_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}|^{2}+|\nabla v_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}|^{2})dx+\frac{3\beta}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}u^{2}_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}v_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}

\displaystyle\leq\lambda_{1,k}b_{1,k}^{2}+\lambda_{2,k}b_{2,k}^{2}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla u_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}|^{2}+|\nabla v_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}|^{2})dx

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left(\mu_{1}u_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}^{4}+\mu_{2}v_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}^{4}+2\rho u_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}^{2}v_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}^{2}\right)dx,

combing with (
5.4
) and (
5.9
), we get

\lambda_{1,k}b_{1,k}^{2}+\lambda_{2,k}b_{2,k}^{2}\backsim b_{1,k}^{\frac{22}{5}}b_{2,k}^{\frac{8}{5}}+b_{1,k}^{\frac{16}{5}}b_{2,k}^{\frac{14}{5}},

thus
\lambda_{1,k}\backsim b_{1,k}^{4}
and
\lambda_{2,k}\backsim b_{1,k}^{4}
when
b_{1,k}\backsim b_{2,k}
.

Denote

\theta_{1,k}=\frac{2\beta^{2}b_{1,k}^{\frac{6}{5}}b_{2,k}^{\frac{4}{5}}}{16^{\frac{2}{5}}\|w\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}},\ \theta_{2,k}=\frac{\beta^{2}b_{1,k}^{\frac{8}{5}}b_{2,k}^{\frac{2}{5}}}{16^{\frac{1}{5}}\|w\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}},\ L_{1,k}=\frac{2\beta^{4}b_{1,k}^{\frac{14}{5}}b_{2,k}^{\frac{6}{5}}}{16^{\frac{3}{5}}\|w\|^{4}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}},\ \ L_{2,k}=\frac{4\beta^{4}b_{1,k}^{\frac{12}{5}}b_{2,k}^{\frac{8}{5}}}{16^{\frac{4}{5}}\|w\|^{4}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}}.

Define

\widetilde{u}_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}=L^{-1}_{1,k}u_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}(\theta^{-1}_{1,k}x),\ \ \widetilde{v}_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}=L^{-1}_{2,k}v_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}(\theta^{-1}_{2,k}x),

then

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla\widetilde{u}_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}|^{2}+|\nabla\widetilde{v}_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}|^{2})dx

(5.13)

\displaystyle=\frac{\theta_{1,k}}{L^{2}_{1,k}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\nabla u_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}|^{2}dx+\frac{\theta_{2,k}}{L^{2}_{2,k}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\nabla v_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}|^{2}dx

\displaystyle\leq\Big[\frac{16^{\frac{4}{5}}\|w\|^{6}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}}{2\beta^{6}b_{1,k}^{\frac{22}{5}}b_{2,k}^{\frac{8}{5}}}+\frac{16^{\frac{2}{5}}\|w\|^{6}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}}{2\beta^{6}b_{1,k}^{\frac{16}{5}}b_{2,k}^{\frac{14}{5}}}\Big]\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\nabla u_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}|^{2}+|\nabla v_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}|^{2})dx

\displaystyle\leq\Big[\frac{16^{\frac{4}{5}}\|w\|^{6}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}}{2\beta^{6}b_{1,k}^{\frac{22}{5}}b_{2,k}^{\frac{8}{5}}}+\frac{16^{\frac{2}{5}}\|w\|^{6}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}}{2\beta^{6}b_{1,k}^{\frac{16}{5}}b_{2,k}^{\frac{14}{5}}}\Big]\left[\frac{3|\beta|}{2}\Big[\big(\frac{2}{3}b^{\frac{3}{2}}_{1,k}+\frac{1}{3}b^{\frac{3}{2}}_{2,k}\big)C^{3}_{3,3}\Big]\right]^{4},

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\widetilde{u}_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}|^{2}+|\widetilde{v}_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}|^{2})dx

\displaystyle=\frac{\theta_{1,k}^{3}}{L^{2}_{1,k}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|u_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}|^{2}dx+\frac{\theta_{2,k}^{3}}{L^{2}_{2,k}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|v_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}|^{2}dx

(5.14)

\displaystyle=\frac{2\|w\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\|w\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}}{\beta^{2}},

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Big(\frac{\theta_{1,k}^{3}}{L^{4}_{1,k}}\mu_{1}u_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}^{4}+\mu_{2}\frac{\theta_{2,k}^{3}}{L^{4}_{2,k}}v_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}^{4}\Big)dx

(5.15)

\displaystyle\quad<\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left(\mu_{1}\widetilde{u}_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}^{4}+\mu_{2}\widetilde{v}_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}^{4}+2\rho\widetilde{u}_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}^{2}\widetilde{v}_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}^{2}\right)dx

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Big(\frac{\theta_{1,k}^{3}}{L^{4}_{1,k}}\mu_{1}u_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}^{4}+\mu_{2}\frac{\theta_{2,k}^{3}}{L^{4}_{2,k}}v_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}^{4}+2\rho u_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}^{2}v_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}^{2}\Big)dx

\displaystyle\quad\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Big(\frac{\theta_{1,k}^{3}}{L^{4}_{1,k}}(\mu_{1}+\rho)u_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}^{4}+(\mu_{2}+\rho)\frac{\theta_{2,k}^{3}}{L^{4}_{2,k}}v_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}^{4}\Big)dx.

By the definition of
\theta_{1,k},\ \theta_{2,k},\ L_{1,k},\ L_{2,k}
, it is easy to see that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\big(\mu_{1}\widetilde{u}_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}^{4}+\mu_{2}\widetilde{v}_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}^{4}+2\rho\widetilde{u}_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}^{2}\widetilde{v}_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}^{2}\big)dx\rightarrow+\infty\ \text{as}\ k\rightarrow+\infty.

(5.16)

From (
5.13
)- (
5.14
), we know that
(\widetilde{u}_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}},\widetilde{v}_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}})
is bounded in
H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\times H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}).
Then, we have

\big(\widetilde{u}_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}(x),\widetilde{v}_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}(x)\big)\rightharpoonup(\bar{u},\bar{v})\neq(0,0),

for some
(\bar{u},\bar{v})\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\times H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
.
Thus, we see that
(\widetilde{u}_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}(x),\widetilde{v}_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}(x))
satisfies

\begin{cases}-\Delta\widetilde{u}_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}+\frac{\lambda_{1,k}}{\theta^{2}_{1,k}}\widetilde{u}_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}=\mu_{1}\frac{L^{2}_{1,k}}{\theta^{2}_{1,k}}\widetilde{u}_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}^{3}+\rho\frac{L^{2}_{2,k}}{\theta^{2}_{1,k}}\widetilde{v}_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}^{2}\widetilde{u}_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}+\beta\frac{L_{2,k}}{\theta^{2}_{1,k}}\widetilde{u}_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}\widetilde{v}_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}},\\
-\Delta\widetilde{v}_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}+\frac{\lambda_{2,k}}{\theta^{2}_{2,k}}\widetilde{v}_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}=\mu_{2}\frac{L^{2}_{2,k}}{\theta^{2}_{2,k}}\widetilde{v}_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}^{3}+\rho\frac{L^{2}_{1,k}}{\theta^{2}_{2,k}}\widetilde{u}_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}^{2}\widetilde{v}_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}+\frac{\beta}{2}\frac{L^{2}_{1,k}}{L_{2,k}\theta^{2}_{2,k}}\widetilde{u}_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}^{2}.\end{cases}

(5.17)

By the definition of
\theta_{1,k},\ \theta_{2,k},\ L_{1,k},\ L_{2,k}
, we have

\frac{L^{2}_{1,k}}{\theta^{2}_{1,k}}=\frac{\beta^{4}b_{1,k}^{\frac{16}{5}}b_{2,k}^{\frac{4}{5}}}{16^{\frac{2}{5}}\|w\|^{4}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}}\rightarrow 0,\ \frac{L^{2}_{2,k}}{\theta^{2}_{2,k}}=\frac{\beta^{4}b_{1,k}^{\frac{8}{5}}b_{2,k}^{\frac{12}{5}}}{16^{\frac{1}{5}}\|w\|^{4}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}}\rightarrow 0,

\frac{L^{2}_{2,k}}{\theta^{2}_{1,k}}=\frac{4\beta^{4}b_{1,k}^{\frac{12}{5}}b_{2,k}^{\frac{8}{5}}}{16^{\frac{4}{5}}\|w\|^{4}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}}\rightarrow 0,\ \ \frac{L^{2}_{1,k}}{\theta^{2}_{2,k}}=\frac{4\beta^{4}b_{1,k}^{\frac{12}{5}}b_{2,k}^{\frac{8}{5}}}{16^{\frac{4}{5}}\|w\|^{4}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}}\rightarrow 0,\ \frac{L_{2,k}}{\theta^{2}_{1,k}}=1,\ \ \frac{L^{2}_{1,k}}{L_{2,k}\theta^{2}_{2,k}}=1,

and there exists
\lambda^{\ast}_{1}>0
and
\lambda^{\ast}_{2}>0
such that

\frac{\lambda_{1,k}}{\theta^{2}_{1,k}}\rightarrow\lambda^{\ast}_{1},\ \frac{\lambda_{2,k}}{\theta^{2}_{2,k}}\rightarrow\lambda^{\ast}_{2}\ \text{as}\ k\rightarrow+\infty.

Therefore,
(\bar{u},\bar{v})
solves that

\begin{cases}-\Delta u+\lambda^{\ast}_{1}u=\beta uv&\text{in}\ \ \mathbb{R}^{3},\\
-\Delta v+\lambda^{\ast}_{2}v=\frac{\beta}{2}u^{2}&\text{in}\ \ \mathbb{R}^{3}.\end{cases}

(5.18)

From Theorem 1.2 of
[
49
]
, we know that when
\lambda^{\ast}_{1}=\lambda^{\ast}_{2}
, then

\big(\sqrt{2}\lambda^{\ast}_{1}\beta^{-1}w((\lambda^{\ast}_{1})^{\frac{1}{2}}x),\beta^{-1}\lambda^{\ast}_{1}w((\lambda^{\ast}_{1})^{\frac{1}{2}}x)\big)

is the unique positive solution of above system, where
w
is the unique positive solution of

-\Delta u+u=u^{2},\ u\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}).

Texting (
5.17
) and (
5.18
) with
\widetilde{u}_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}-\bar{u}
,
\widetilde{v}_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}-\bar{v}
respectively, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\nabla

\displaystyle(\widetilde{u}_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}-\bar{u})|^{2}dx+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(\frac{\lambda_{1,k}}{\theta^{2}_{1,k}}\widetilde{u}_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}-\lambda^{\ast}_{1}\bar{u})(\widetilde{u}_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}-\bar{u})

(5.19)

\displaystyle-\beta\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(\widetilde{u}_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}\widetilde{v}_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}-\bar{u}\bar{v})(\widetilde{u}_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}-\bar{u})

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\nabla(\widetilde{u}_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}-\bar{u})|^{2}dx+\lambda^{\ast}_{1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\widetilde{u}_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}-\bar{u}|^{2}=o_{k}(1),

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\nabla

\displaystyle(\widetilde{v}_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}-\bar{v})|^{2}dx+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(\frac{\lambda_{2,k}}{\theta^{2}_{2,k}}\widetilde{v}_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}-\lambda^{\ast}_{2}\bar{v})(\widetilde{v}_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}-\bar{v})

(5.20)

\displaystyle-\frac{\beta}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(\widetilde{u}^{2}_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}-\bar{u}^{2})(\widetilde{v}_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}-\bar{v})

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\nabla(\widetilde{v}_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}-\bar{v})|^{2}dx+\lambda^{\ast}_{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\widetilde{v}_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}-\bar{v}|^{2}=o_{k}(1).

Therefore

\displaystyle\frac{2(\lambda^{\ast}_{1})^{\frac{1}{2}}\|w\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}}{\beta^{2}}=\|\sqrt{2}\lambda^{\ast}_{1}\beta^{-1}w((\lambda^{\ast}_{1})^{\frac{1}{2}}x)\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}=\|\bar{u}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle=\lim_{k\rightarrow+\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\widetilde{u}_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}|^{2}dx=\lim_{k\rightarrow+\infty}\frac{\theta_{1,k}^{3}}{L^{2}_{1,k}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|u_{b_{1,k},b_{2,k}}|^{2}dx=\frac{2\|w\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}}{\beta^{2}},
